# MASTER PLAN — Single Source of Truth

The only planning document in this repository. **§1** the standing rules, **§2** the codebase map,
**§3** what is real and what is not, **§4** the work order, **§5** instructions for the OPEN items,
**§6** defects and watchlist, **§7** the record (never renumbered).

**How to work:** take the next unfinished §4 item, read its §5 section, build it, run the §1.10
ladder, commit it as one bounded change naming the item. Never start an item whose prerequisites
are not done. **When an item closes:** its §4 row says so in one line, its §5 section is DELETED,
and the record goes in §7.

**This file was compressed 2026-08-31 under rule 11** (4,387 → under 1,000 lines). Every §7
record keeps its NUMBER and its finding/number/lesson in one line; the uncompressed narrative for
every record lives in git history immediately before the compression commit. Code comments cite
§7.N — the numbering is load-bearing and never changes.

---

## 1. Rules of engagement

Standing user directives. Not suggestions.

1. **Every asset price is the result of real supply/demand clearing.** OAS, discount margin,
   yield, P/E are *statistics derived from a cleared price*, never the mechanism that sets it.
   One exception: central-bank **administered** rates (SRF / ON RRP), where a posted rate with a
   real quantity response is itself the real-world mechanism.

2. **No caps, floors, ceilings or rescales** except genuinely mathematical ones (a price cannot be
   negative; finiteness). If a number explodes, the mechanism that should compensate is broken:
   find it and fix it at the root. Never clamp the symptom.

3. **"1$ is 1$":** every dollar traces to a named counterparty. The recurring anti-pattern to hunt
   and kill is **two disconnected representations of one real thing** — a real cleared ledger and
   a parallel formula that overwrites or ignores it.

4. **No real-world data, and no real-world OUTCOMES either.** No real tickers, company names,
   observed prices or copied spread tables. A real-world *primitive* is fine (a tax rate, a
   replacement rate, a regulatory ratio, a haircut); a real-world *equilibrium* is not — dollar
   invoicing dominance, a 24% foreign ownership share, a fixed CB market share are RESULTS of
   histories this simulation does not have. Import one and the model can never tell you anything
   about it, because you assumed the answer.

5. **Target allocations are long-term policy guides only.** What a participant buys each week is a
   tactical decision from real characteristics, never the target mechanically.

6. **Long tests are end-of-project only.** During development: `npx tsc --noEmit`,
   `bash scripts/check-hygiene.sh`, `npm test`, and short scratchpad probes.

7. **One bounded, verified commit per slice.** Never one large unreviewable change.

8. **Reflect the real-world mechanism.** When in doubt, the answer is how it actually works, with
   real named counterparties. Ask before large scope decisions.

9. **Periodicity is part of the number.** Every rate, growth figure, flow and index carries a
   period; mixing two silently is one of the most common defects here. **Confirm the period at the
   source and name it in the identifier** (`...WeeklyUSD`, `...Annual`, `...YoY`). A price level is
   part of a number too (§7.149), and so is a unit of meaning: a field named USD is not a share
   (§7.165). Displayed values show MoM and YoY; where history is short, show the level — a missing
   change is information, a fabricated one is a lie.

10. **The simulation is a partial world — do not chase every moved number.** Whole systems are
    still missing, so counts shift for non-economic reasons. Attribute a moved baseline BRIEFLY
    (one cheap A/B at most), record it with its owner, and move on.

11. **Brevity in comments and in this file.** A comment earns its place by saying what the code
    cannot: why a constant has its value, what a non-obvious mechanism is, what was tried and
    failed. Same for this document: every record is the finding, the number and the lesson. No
    information is dropped; the narrative is. **Clean up as you go.**

12. **Do not evaluate market behaviour mid-update.** BUILD THE WHOLE THING before measuring.
    Numbers taken halfway through describe an economy that does not exist yet. **Measure once, at
    the end.** The narrow exceptions are structural: `tsc`/`build`, and a targeted probe that a
    mechanism you JUST wrote is wired. **The harness may be deliberately red mid-project.**

13. **Ownership, prices, quantities and capacities are OUTCOMES.** Never impose a share, a price, a
    flow or a capacity a mechanism should produce. **A residual with no holder is a defect, not a
    boundary** — a named gap is legitimate only with a size, an owner and a scheduled closing
    slice; otherwise it is a plug with paperwork.

14. **Every flow has two sides, and both legs go in the same pass.** A security movement has a cash
    leg; a derivative has a counterparty with finite capacity; a payment leaving one book arrives
    on another. **A one-sided flow is a defect even when nothing fails and every test passes.**
    Building a two-sided flow one side at a time is how a leg goes missing.

15. **A bound is not a price.** All markets clear through the clearing engine: a participant posts
    a reservation, a `fullSizeStatRange`, holding/net-purchase bounds, and the solve clears at the
    **saturation point** if demand cannot absorb the float. Never park a print on a clamp and call
    it a price (§7.21, §7.75, §7.130, §7.132); the subtlest form is a bound that LOOKS like dollars
    and is a percentage wearing dollars' clothes.

16. **Delivery.** One bounded commit per slice, pushed. Commit messages and §7 records state what
    changed, WHY, and the measured numbers, for a reader who was not here. **No model identifiers
    in any committed artifact.** Do not open a PR unless asked.

17. **The targeted-change test.** Adding a product line, a lead time, a revenue rule or a fund type
    must be ONE targeted change: a registry entry, or one profile module. All DATA about what is
    made or who is acting lives in a registry; all BEHAVIOR that varies by kind lives in a profile
    behind a dispatch table. **A stage may not switch on an industry, sector or entity type.**

18. **Model updates come first; a misbehaving NUMBER is not a work item.** The priority is always
    the missing MECHANISM; scoping a project around closing a number is how a model gets tuned
    instead of built. Evidence: §7.146–163 moved the week-10 price print −25.5% → +4.3% with none
    of the changes targeting it. A row whose content is "this number is wrong" is a CONSEQUENCE
    row and its evidence must be re-measured before scoping, never inherited. **The other half
    (§7.179):** when a number is so far out it BLOCKS unrelated mechanism work, closing the gap IS
    mechanism work — find which missing mechanism the number is the accumulated cost of, build that.

19. **THE FEWEST PRIMITIVES THAT GENERATE THE WORLD.** (The most basic rule; 1, 4 and 13 are
    corollaries.) A number is a legitimate primitive **only if no mechanism in the model can produce
    it**: **TECHNOLOGY** (what a process physically takes), **PREFERENCE** (time and risk), and
    **POLICY** (what an institution chooses). Everything else is an OUTCOME, and a stated value for
    it is a defect with a scheduled death.
    - **RESOLUTION** parameters (strata count, grid size) are numerical choices; the test is
      INVARIANCE. **SHAPE** parameters (a Pareto alpha, tier shares, an MPC ladder) are claims
      about THE ANSWER. **TRUE** primitives are the three kinds above, and there should be few.
    - The count of stated shape parameters measures how much mechanism is missing. Keep it falling
      (§5-DIST-P holds the scoreboard). Where an aggregate sits far from a threshold its own
      mechanism turns on, that mechanism is SWITCHED OFF and a constant is standing in for it.
    - **The corollary that costs most:** a shape parameter STANDS IN FOR a missing mechanism —
      deleting it before the mechanism exists makes the model wrong, not more bottom-up (§7.158,
      §7.177). The order is forced.

1.20 **NEVER ROLL BACK.** When a change makes a print worse, the answer is never to put the old
    number back. A derivation that replaced an invented constant does not become wrong because the
    world it now describes is uglier — the ugliness was there, and the constant was covering it
    (§7.209 named this rule). **A bad print is a finding, not a regression.** The only thing that
    may be undone is a change WRONG on its own terms — a ratchet, a resolution artefact, a broken
    identity — and that is a fix, not a rollback. This binds hardest at the end of a project, when
    one number is embarrassing and the old constant is one line away.

1.21 **DEPTH IS UNTOUCHABLE; REPRESENTATION IS FREE (user, 2026-09-01).** Mechanisms, economics,
    and named boundaries never change under the performance campaign. Storage layout, float
    summation order, parallel decomposition, and scaffolding deletion are the campaign's to
    decide without asking. Only an economics change (a T+1-class decision) goes to the user.

1.22 **STRONGEST GATE THE CHANGE ADMITS.** Byte-identical 4/13wk dumps wherever possible. A
    change that is inherently a relabel (a reordered fold, a dump-format flip) is a DECLARED
    §7.313 canonical re-baseline, named in the record. Declared reorder points are listed in
    §4.C; a reorder never lands undeclared.

1.23 **NO FORECAST WITHOUT A FALSIFICATION TEST.** A record may state an expectation only with
    the measurement that would kill it. Standing kill test for a grind track: two consecutive
    slices yielding under half their named term → stop the track, re-profile, reorder.
    (Adopted after §7.316's "the step change is the parallel path" was measured false by
    §7.326: the parallel arc returned zero while the dismissed serial grind kept paying.)

1.24 **NEW COLUMN STORES ARE SAB-BACKED WITH COPY-ON-GROW.** Every store built under §4.C
    allocates SharedArrayBuffer-backed columns so later sharding ships nothing (§7.326: the
    clone transport ate the win), and every growth path copies (§7.309's wipe).

1.25 **A RUN IS THE LAST GATE, NOT THE FIRST (user, 2026-09-02: "really minimize the use of
    tests").** At most ONE 13-week harness per commit and no experiment runs; the cheap gates
    (`tsc`, lint, hygiene, the unit tests) and reading the code come first, a two-week probe
    (`WEEKS=2`) is allowed before the one run for a slice that touches settlement, and a
    defect the run finds is diagnosed by reading and by the instruments already in the tree
    before any second run. A second run on one commit is recorded with its reason (§7.372,
    §7.376). Adding an instrument the next run carries for free beats a run of its own.

### 1.10 Verification ladder

| Command | Cost | When |
|---|---|---|
| `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm run build` | seconds | any time, always before a commit |
| `npm run lint`, `npm test` | seconds | any time; both gate CI, both ratchets |
| `bash scripts/check-hygiene.sh` | seconds | any time; fails if a second script appears |
| `PROFILE=1 WEEKS=4 npx tsx scripts/harness.ts` | ~30 s | a profile is not a run (§1.25): allowed any time; §7.383 is the current read |
| `WEEKS=2 SHOCKS=0 VERBOSE=1 npx tsx scripts/harness.ts` | ~50 s | the probe before the one run, for a slice that touches settlement (§1.25) |
| `WEEKS=13 SHOCKS=0 VERBOSE=1 NODE_OPTIONS=--max-semi-space-size=64 npx tsx scripts/harness.ts` | ~5 min | **THE ONE RUN per commit** (§1.25) — the quarterly clocks, the audit families, the scoreboard to record |
| `WEEKS=60 SHOCKS=1` (npm run verify: 243 wk + batteries) | ~25 min+ | **END OF PROJECT ONLY** (rule 12; FINALIZATION Part VI) |

`NODE_OPTIONS=--max-semi-space-size=64` is worth 7.5% (allocation-bound, §7.213); wired into
`npm run verify`/`npm run profile`. `UNIVERSE_SCALE<1` is iteration speed ONLY — the rule-19
invariance test FAILS on the current world (§7.228). A 10-week probe samples ONE SEASON (IND18);
price behaviour is judged on whole years.

## 2. Codebase map

### 2.1 The weekly pipeline (`src/engine/simulation/`)

`core.ts` builds a `WeeklyStepContext` (`stages/context.ts` — one shared mutable context) and runs
~50 stages in order:

| Group | Stages | Owns |
|---|---|---|
| Macro & credit | 01-macro-feedback, 02-region-macro, 02b-bank-diversification, labor-market, prime-brokerage | Contagion; region evolution and the administered policy rate (no curve); per-bank flow ledgers, GC repo, deposit-vs-MMF split; labour; broker lines |
| Real economy | 03-category-demand, 04-input-output, trade-settlement, goods-arrival, sourcing-intent, freight-clearing, 05-unit-bidding, 06-fx-and-trade, 07-commodities | C+G plus the Leontief intermediate half (§7.272); input clearing; invoices; arrivals; sourcing/freight; **THE goods auction** (five books per sub-unit, per-lot settlement, contracts, capex bids, distribution channel); FX conversion; commodity spot |
| Financial books | 07b corp bonds, 07c sovereigns (curve's only owner), 07d loans, 07f bills+CP, securities-lending, 07e equity, **derivatives** (the clearing phase: swaps, CDS, futures) | Each a thin adapter over the clearing engine. **ONE derivative stage (`stages/derivatives.ts`, §7.382) runs every class the registry names, in its order, at its phase — the class MARKETS (`stages/derivative-markets/`, one module each behind `DERIVATIVE_MARKETS`) keep only demand measurement + clearing and read the STANDING INDEX (`domain/derivatives/standing-book.ts`: one walk of the book per settle, a lookup per question); every contract lives on THE ONE DERIVATIVE BOOK (`GameState.derivativesBook`) and passes through `derivative-lifecycle.ts` — legs, maturity, credit events, counterparty close-out, capacity, margin — driven by the class profiles in `domain/derivatives/` (§7.337)** |
| Settle & report | repo-collateral-reconcile, holdings-writeback, 08-company-fundamentals, settlement, sme-pools, hc-lifecycle, insurance-and-pensions, index-calculation, etf-flows, household-balance-sheet, estate-resolution, 09-concentration-risk, 10-mergers, **management-review**, **derivatives-post-settlement** (the FX forwards, hedging the cross-border book settlement left behind), fx-clearing, money-fund-income, bill-accretion, sovereign-calendar, 11-fiscal, labor-reconciliation, **overdraft-sweep** (§7.357), settlement-close, **bank-funding-close** (§7.340/§7.351), **bank-resolution**, central-bank, **settlement-funding** (the third cycle, §7.362), 12-portfolio, register-consolidation, **news-derivation**, 13-news (`institutional-marking` is deleted, §7.374 — sizing reads the live register) | Per-company week (order-invariant sharded kernel, entity-scoped RNG §7.223; rules in `domain/company-week/` §7.238); lifecycle; **settlement (intraday + close cycles; comp.cash has ONE mover — settlement, §7.263)**; SME pools; M&A; the sovereign coupon calendar (§7.208, one party-keyed ledger); measured GDP/CPI, issuance; ETF flows and the share book; FX hedging (the FX_FORWARD market half; the contract is the one lifecycle's); **bank resolution (§7.339: PCA close on the final sheets, least-cost P&A to the strongest peer — rules in `domain/bank-resolution.ts`, the whole-sheet transfer in `engine/ledger/bank-transfer.ts`, shared with 10-mergers)**; **management review (§5-MGMT slice 1: every entity's brain drawn once at entry; a firm's management replaced after a §7.138 year of failed quarterly reviews against its own baseline and its peers' median, §7.344)**; marks |

**`stages/financial-clearing-engine.ts`** — the generic cap-free double auction: real demand
schedules, exact piecewise-linear segment walk, **saturation clearing**; `statKind` orients it;
flat typed arrays, worker-shardable byte-identically. Every asset class is a thin adapter owning
participants, reservations, sizes and the float. `unsoldStaysWithHolder` rations BOTH sides (stock
books); flow books keep a dealer residual (real outside order). Read §7.16 and §7.21 first.

Other engine files: `initialization.ts` (must seed the shape the engine produces — §7.4),
`shared-helpers.ts`, `equity-valuation.ts` (the ONE answer to what a share is worth), `credit.ts`,
`merger.ts`, `trade.ts`, `constants.ts`.

### 2.2 Engine support (`src/engine/`)

`companyGenerator.ts`, `pricing.ts` (display/derivation only), `nelsonSiegel.ts`, `blackScholes.ts`,
`rng.ts` (all draws; seed+position on GameState; `beginEntityScope` keys a draw to its entity —
§7.223), `newsGenerator.ts`, `formatters.ts` (`formatPercent` requires `isDecimal`), `macro/`,
`bootstrap/` (all generated from primitives; seed investment fixed point §7.199).

**`ledger/` — the money primitive (§7.230, §7.242, §7.275–276).** `post(payer, payee, amountUSD,
reason)` is the one write path — there is no unbacked exception any more (§5-CLOSE: a movement with
no counterparty is `defect()`); `pay()` throws on NaN/negative; `bookPnL(bank, amount, reason)` is the one bank-P&L write (PNL_TRACE=1);
`payment-category.ts` classifies every reason at intern time (unclassified = harness violation).
`check-hygiene.sh` fails the build on a money-field write outside the ledger, both forms.
**The wire ledgers (§5-WIRES):** `ledger/wire.ts` (the columnar `WireJournal`, `wire()`, `summarizeWires`),
`ledger/party.ts` (a party is an int32), `ledger/holdings-ledger.ts` (`transferHolding`/`issueHolding`/
`retireHolding`/`scaleHoldings`/`clearedBookDelta`), `ledger/tranche-ledger.ts` (`issueTranche`/
`retireTranche`/`retireLadderFace`/`commitLadder`/`rebuildLadder`/`moveFacilityLender`/`seedLadder`),
`ledger/goods-ledger.ts` (`deliverGoods`/`receiveInputLot`/`settleOutputInventory`/…), `ledger/accounts.ts`
(the persistent accounts on `v2.accounts` — companies since §7.384: `cashOf`, `openAccount`, the opening-cash stash —
(the account store: `buildAccountMirror`, `applySettledRow`, `projectBooks`, `compareToBooks`). The
payment journal (`stages/settlement.ts`) carries a settle week per row (`pay({settleWeek})`,
`rowDue`, the carry). **The audit (`src/engine/audit/`):** money M1–M7, ownership O1–O5, prices
P1–P3, cross-market X1–X2, accounts F1–F3, names N1–N2, wires W1–W4; the harness runs it weekly
and prints the scoreboard.
Also in `simulation/`: `stage-deps.ts` (STAGE_TRACE=1 records context reads/writes;
`DELIBERATE_PIPELINE_FIELDS` annotates every deliberate backward edge, remainder ratchets at ZERO
— §7.278), `burn-in.ts` (SEED_BURN_IN=auto|n, off by default — runs the engine to a CONVERGENCE test on the watched quantities, calendar continuous; SEED_BURN_IN_TRACE=1|2 the trace every 4 weeks / every week — §7.345). **The §7.345 cause instruments:** DEFAULT_TRACE=1 (every firm death with its books at the moment), LABOR_CAUSES=1 (the week's layoffs by rule — distress / affordability / growth — with quits and vacancies, per region), `bank-identity-trace.ts`
(BANK_IDENTITY_TRACE=1, the per-stage identity residual instrument — §7.252, §7.259).

### 2.3 Domain (`src/domain/`)

The registry is the single data owner (rule 17): `industry-registry.ts` (sub-unit specs — physics,
buyer mix, recipe BOM, seasonality, financing, consumption intensity) with derived views.
Instruments/books: banking, company, institutions, primary-market, call-protection, repo,
securities-lending, commercial-paper, prime-brokerage, dealer-desk, dealer-derivatives (the FX
desk's inventory only), etf, indexes, distribution, volatility. **`derivatives/` — THE ONE
DERIVATIVE LAYER (§7.337, rule 17 at full scale):** `contract.ts` (one contract shape, one party
encoding, the §7.241 standing-cover index written once), `profile.ts` (a class IS its legs: a
periodic leg or a mark leg, an event termination, a close-out; PFE add-on and margin as facts),
`hedging.ts` (the one walk-away arithmetic every hedger prices with), `registry.ts` (the
dispatch table + ONE desk-capacity budget across every class; `DERIVATIVE_CLASS_IDS` is the
order the one stage runs them in), `standing-book.ts` (the live book indexed in one walk —
cover per side and PFE charge per party, §7.382), `classes/{irs,cds,commodity-future,
fx-forward}.ts`. **Adding a derivative = one profile module + one registry line + one market
module under `stages/derivative-markets/` + its line in `DERIVATIVE_MARKETS`.**
Trade/FX: global-goods, trade-invoice, invoice-currency, cross-border, fx-market, currency, carrier.
Households/state: region-macro, household-credit, estate, government, central-bank, geography,
game-state, defect (GUARD's loud failures).
STRUCT objects: `company-week/` (seven pure flat-input rule objects, 72 tests, §7.238 —
capital-programme carries §7.288's derived growth-funding cap), **`preferences.ts` (§5-BRAINS:
the two preference primitives — patience as a horizon in weeks, risk aversion relative to 1 —
drawn once per deciding entity from its own stream; adaptive expectations at that horizon; the
median brain is the stated rule, §7.344)**, `assets/` (four-taxonomy
reconciliation), `government-entity.ts` (the one budget decomposition; the stance reads it
§7.280), `collateral.ts` (one pledge tolerance), `fund.ts`, `sme-pool.ts`, `sovereign-id.ts`
(the ONE GOV id format; `isBillBucketKey`), `institution-profiles.ts` (entityType facts registry;
`mandatePctOf`), `units.ts` (branded number families, applied seam by seam), `bank-book.ts`
(BankingSectorView readonly typing — §7.279).

### 2.4 UI and the harness

**The product UI is AURORA** (`src/ui/`, §5-AU, §7.342/§7.346/§7.348): `Aurora.tsx` the phone shell,
`world.ts` the resolver and the tape, `objects/` the eighteen object modules, `functions/` the
twenty-three functions; `src/main.tsx` routes `#bench` to the bench and everything else to Aurora.
The older `src/components/` tree was DELETED (user-ordered, 2026-09-01; f277780) — recoverable
only from git history — and `src/App.tsx` is the BENCH SHELL (§7.333):
Run/Stop/Restart/Copy over a copy-pasteable report (cores, crossOriginIsolated, heap, per-week
ms, steady MEDIAN + mean, per-stage means over non-outlier weeks, world-digest checkpoints at
weeks 1/5/10/20/50). The browser build WORKS and DEPLOYS (it was broken since native-kernels
landed): `vite.config.ts` aliases `node:module` to `src/shims/node-module-browser.ts`, defines
`process.env` as `{}` (every engine flag reads off), base `./`; `dist/` is tsc-excluded. Deploy
chain, all automatic on push to `main`: `.github/workflows/deploy-pages.yml` → GitHub Pages at
**https://gunsandsalvi.github.io/Test-app/** (PWA-installable: `public/manifest.webmanifest` +
icons); `.github/workflows/android-apk.yml` → a signed TWA APK on the fixed release
**apk-latest** (rebuilds only on `android/**` changes — the APK wraps the URL, so bench changes
reach the installed app through Pages alone). `public/coi-serviceworker.min.js` grants
cross-origin isolation on Pages (unhides SharedArrayBuffer);
`gunsandsalvi/gunsandsalvi.github.io` (separate repo, user-created) serves
`.well-known/assetlinks.json` vouching for `android/keystore.p12` so the app runs fullscreen.
Browser worker pool: `stages/clearing-worker-pool-web.ts` + `clearing-worker-web.ts` (§7.334).
UI still reads `GameState` only. **One harness:** `scripts/harness.ts` via `npm run verify` — every
check, battery, profiler as modules on one pass. Checks: NaN purity, ownership/holdings
conservation, per-bank identity, CCP and unresolved residuals, **the audit families M/O/P/X/F/N/W**
(`src/engine/audit/`, §5-CLOSE and §5-WIRES, every week; M7 accounts = books, §7.377 — there is NO
boundary party and no declared frontier any more: CLOSE C3 deleted `UNMODELED`, §7.351, and a
flow to or from nobody fails the week it appears), NAV identity, damper binding (decomposed by
book, §7.288 — binds 0 since NO CAPS, §7.365), bank capital/NIM bands (all regions),
unemployment bands, shock batteries. Instruments (env-gated): FP=1 fingerprint,
STAGE_TRACE, BANK_IDENTITY_TRACE, NIM_TRACE, BANKCAP, DESK_TRACE, BOOKTRACE, SPIRAL/
SPIRAL_PRICES/PX_FOCUS, SEED_RECON, CARRIER/FREIGHT_TRACE, OD_TRACE, PNL_TRACE, BYPASS_TRACE,
RECON_TRACE, CAPEX_TRACE, BOUNDARY_TRACE, LEFTOVER_TRACE, EQ_CONS_TRACE, HH_EQ_TRACE.

**One TEST TREE:** `test/` holds PURE-FUNCTION tests over `domain/` only (no engine run) —
123 tests; hygiene enforces the boundary. **The gates, every one a ratchet (may fall, never
rise):** lint = `tsc --noEmit` (strict) + ESLint on this repo's paid-for defect classes
(ceiling 354 in `package.json`, 349 measured at handoff); hygiene budgets: assignment-form money
writes 2, spread-form 16, asset-literal comparisons 54, the LEDGER_OWNED money-field rule, the
import boundaries, the derived-quantity rule (`.marketCap|.totalDebt|.totalAssetsUSD` read only
in the read modules, §5-WIRES D), test purity. CI runs lint+hygiene+tests on push/PR; the harness is deliberately
NOT in CI (red mid-project by design).

## 3. Current state

**Bottom-up today.** Category demand (C+G + Leontief intermediate) → input-output → stage 05's
auctions with named counterparties, per-lot provenance, settled sales AS revenue, capex as real
bids capped by real financing (§7.288), a distribution channel priced off physics. Commodities
from extraction cost. Every financial book clears on one engine, with the primary market and
named bank desks as ordinary participants. Per-bank balance sheets, real CB facilities, itemized
lending with endogenous money, real issuance, remittance loop, measured CPI, single-owner curve,
OIS on the cleared repo print. Private lifecycle (LP capital, marks at the public multiple).
Households at corporate depth: cohorts, real ageing, cross-sections where decisions are
nonlinear, a labour market summing real employers. SME pools per (region × industry). One
settlement layer with two cycles; **deposits evolve by settlement alone** (the 02b reconcile
deleted at a measured zero — §7.288). Default resolution with real estates (assets sold to named
peers, real invoice receivables — §7.286). The UNMODELED boundary is silent at 60 weeks (§7.287).

**The wire era (§5-WIRES, §7.367–378).** Every move of money, paper, face and goods is a NUMBERED
WIRE in one columnar journal (`ledger/wire.ts`); the holdings, tranche and lot stores are sealed
behind ledgers whose functions demand the wire (`holdings-ledger`, `tranche-ledger`, `goods-ledger`);
births and deaths are wires; the derived quantities named by D are reads (`marketCapOf`,
`totalDebtOf`/`ladderTotalUSD`, `loanBooksOf`, `institutionTotalAssetsUSD`); the payment journal's
rows are DATED and a pass settles what is due (corporate tax is a wire to the treasury dated at the
quarter); an ACCOUNT STORE (`ledger/accounts.ts`) applies every settled row by one rule and projects
the books from it, the households' money landing on their banks at once (no transit). The harness
prints the closed-model scoreboard every week; the money family (M1–M7) is CLEAN, "the money that is
not anyone's" is 0.00B. **Cost:** ~4.4 s a week at week 4 (§7.383; was 9 s and rising at §7.380),
05 a third of it and 08 a sixth; a 13-week harness ~1.5 minutes on this box.

**Still formula-driven, each with an owner:** the insurer's claims loss ratio and the card
operating cost (IND-R4, gated on missing mechanisms); the corporate tax BASE (TAXR).

**Real but structurally undersupplied.** Honest auctions on an asset universe that does not match
the money pointed at it — half the damper count is the small-cap equity tail (§7.288). SCALE grows
the universe; a correct auction over a short float still gives a wrong price.

## 5. Instructions for the open items

### STRUCT — invariants by construction *(§7.229–242, 274–279, 290 — CLOSED as a project; what
survives is the standing loop)*

Every finite item is done (§7.290): the ledger owns money end to end (§7.288 deleted the
reconcile; the `Account` end-state noun is stated in `engine/ledger/balance.ts`); the four
instrument taxonomies derive from one superset with the registries as the fact-dispatch path
(ratchet 64 → 56); the institution registry carries ten per-kind facts; the labor-demand and
bank-pricing rules joined `domain/` bit-exact with tests. **Deferred, each by its own recorded
rule, to be taken when its gate opens:** the `Money<C>` brand at `pay()` (units.ts's seam rule —
a brand with unbranded producers is theater; the every-call-site window §7.277 named has passed,
so it lands with the columnar payment-journal conversion); the nominal BankBook split (the
readonly view already made the field-poke class uncompilable; a brand buys nothing measured
today); the `details`-bag discriminated union (its 30 consumers are ALL in `src/components` —
AU deletes them, so the union lands with AU's rebuild, not before it).

**The STANDING loop (not a finite item):** when touching a stage that still computes inline
(07f, pe-lifecycle, 11-fiscal, etf-flows are the biggest), extract the rule block first — extract
→ compile → **fingerprint (FP=1, fresh baseline per extraction)** → test → commit; keep every
extracted object flat-input (§7.228). Three of seven kernel extractions failed the fingerprint on
arithmetic reordering alone (§7.238).

### P1 — periodicity and units *(standing sweep)*

The two named renames are done (§7.277). Remainder: walk every rate/flow/index at the source when
touched; the `historicalInflation`/`historicalZeroCurves` one-week lag is documented at the type.
Display: one shared helper, MoM+YoY, period labelled. Aurora inherits it.

### MNC — multinational production *(item 1)*

Every firm is single-region while intra-firm trade is ~a third of real world trade — here zero.
**The design (rule 19 — the fewest primitives): a SUBSIDIARY IS A COMPANY.** The model already
has everything a foreign plant needs — production, labour, books, births, contracts, invoices,
FX — so MNC adds a LINK and a DECISION, never a second production machinery:
1. **The ownership link**: a subsidiary is a real company in region B whose equity the parent
   holds (`parentTicker` + a real register row through the ownership machinery, OWN7-conform).
   Consolidation is a VIEW (parent's earnings + its share of subsidiary earnings), never a
   second set of books.
2. **The FDI decision**: a firm persistently paying more to LAND its exports in region B than a
   local producer's cost (XB3a's landed-cost machinery, measured over the §7.138-style sustained
   window) births a subsidiary there through the existing birth machinery, funded by the
   parent's own cash crossing the FX book as a real payment — the financing decision first, the
   proceeds land, the plant follows (§7.288's discipline).
3. **Intra-firm trade**: supply contracts between parent and subsidiary through the existing
   world contract book — the intra-firm share then EMERGES from who owns whom. The transfer
   price is the contract's struck price (TAXR later makes it a priced choice).
4. **Repatriation**: subsidiary dividends flow to the parent's account through the FX
   conversion path — structural FX exposure the hedging desks' client book then really carries.
**Verify:** FDI responds to landed-cost differentials; an intra-firm trade share EMERGES; one FX
move changes a multinational's consolidated earnings through both channels.

### DYN — entry, exit, industry structure *(item 2)*

Concentration as an outcome. **Entry:** sustained high category margins attract entrants through
HC's birth machinery — entry is DIST's reinjection at the absorbing barrier, do not build it
twice. **Exit:** sustained losses idle capacity (mothballed, restartable) then scrap it — the
missing half of every capacity cycle. **Structure as strategy:** a firm repeatedly burned by
contract breaks (IND11's measured record) integrates upstream; IND7's antitrust is the regulator.
**Folded:** `NAMED_TIER_REVENUE_SHARE = 0.6` states a cut point the Pareto tail already decides —
the naming threshold becomes a property OF the distribution.
**Verify:** category margins mean-revert through entry/exit; capacity leaves in downturns and its
absence is visible in supply; integration follows measured failure.

### PROD — firm productivity and innovation *(item 3)*

`rndExpense` buys nothing today. R&D becomes a real investment with uncertain outcomes — process
(unit cost falls) or product (a new line through the registry) — and diffusion erodes any lead
(rule 4: temporary advantage, never permanent monopoly). Firm-level dispersion plus reallocation
through IND6's price competition is the growth engine.
**Verify:** R&D intensity predicts outcomes noisily; aggregate growth decomposes into within-firm
and reallocation terms; cutting all R&D flattens growth over years.

### CRE — commercial property and leases *(item 4)*

Space as a produced long-lived asset (the construction sector builds it), owned by landlords,
rented on multi-year leases that are real tenant liabilities, financed by CRE loans on named
banks' books — the classic systemic channel (vacancy → landlord default → bank capital).
**Verify:** rents clear on vacancy; lease obligations on tenant books; a CRE bust marks bank
capital through named loans.

### TAXR — corporate tax, really *(item 5)*

Tax depreciation schedules against book (the investment incentive), loss carryforwards (receipts
fall FASTER than profits in downturns; a recovering firm pays nothing for years), and — after
MNC — profit booked where rates are lower.
**Verify:** receipts more cyclical than profits; carryforwards revive and expire; investment
timing responds to depreciation treatment.

### SCALE — universe scale-up *(item 6)*

**★ PARKED — LATER, TBD (user, 2026-09-01 evening; §7.336). Do not resume without the user's
go.** Frozen state: Stage I CLOSED (§7.332); Stage II store on trust (§7.329) with four history
fields columnar (§7.330–331). **Exact resume point: productLines** (63 read sites / 18 files,
§7.311 staging: store + dual-write + check → readers file-by-file → declared delete), then

**§4.C — THE END-TO-END COLUMNAR PROGRAM (user-approved 2026-09-01). THE WORK ORDER OF RECORD
for this campaign, superseding the 7-phase list and the B1–B5 sketch below.** One program:
columns become the AUTHORITY for every family of state, end to end; sharding follows only where
columnar; native and demolition last. Rules §1.21–1.24 bind throughout. Battery + §7 record at
every stage boundary. Baseline at adoption: **1,305 ms/wk** (§7.327's battery — the §4.C text
first cited 1,394, a context-loss error corrected here: §7.327's five slices were already
landed and battery-verified when this program was written).

- **Stage I — adapter/apply column programs.** LARGELY DONE before this program was written
  (§7.327's five slices, −89 ms/wk: 07e dense, corp-action walks fused, consolidateRegister
  epoch map, accumulateShard hoists, accrual hoists; plus Phase 5 slice 1, firm input
  intensities memoized, gated 4/13wk). REMAINING: 07b/07d dense demand builds (73.6/68.4 ms/wk
  at the §7.327 re-profile); the ClearingResult int-indexed interface flip (~19 ms/wk,
  §7.311-style staging); adapters emitting into packClearing's typed arrays directly (deleting
  the object-participants layer); the corp-action walk's ~17 ms remainder. All byte-gated.
  Remaining estimate −60…−100 (kill test §1.23).
- **Stage II — the COMPANY ROW STORE (the spine; §7.317's last big flip).** Mirror +
  COMPANY_SYNC_CHECK; ~100 scalar lanes; sub-stores one at a time (productLines chains,
  output/WIP inventories, construction queue, bounded histories as rings, bankBalanceSheet
  lanes); readers file-by-file (the front pass ~36 ms, buildFrontSeam ~25 and buildBackLanes
  ~10 DIE — kernels read the store); writers flip; comp objects become week-end dirty-only
  views (§7.315's law); STATE_DUMP flips to canonical-from-columns — DECLARED, one re-baseline.
  Dissolves the §7.312 clone-aliasing and §7.320 seam-validity classes (one representation).
  Estimate −150…−300. Prerequisite for III and VI.
- **Stage III — back kernel completed on workers.** Rows in SAB, so A AND B run on workers over
  row ranges (the §7.326 crossing object never exists), redemptions on main, post writes
  columns; per-shard journals folded PER KEY unordered — declared reorder point, canonical
  gate. Profile firms main-side; intern seeding as §7.326. GO/NO-GO: the codebase's first
  measured-positive parallel number, or Stage VI is cancelled and the program stays serial.
- **Stage IV — stage 05 columnar (~381 ms).** Invoice book columnar; the CONTRACT book as rows
  (settle's ~8 ms/wk horizon growth capped); buyers walk on lanes; demand/supply plans emitted
  as columns; aggregate-payers on interned pids. Estimate −120…−200.
- **Stage V — entity store + the ~490 ms tail.** Entity scalar lanes; mark/NAV/fx/repo/ETF
  passes flipped; region categoryDemand columns; remaining ctx maps as lanes; strictly
  profiler-ordered. Estimate −80…−150.
- **Stage VI — shard the columnar stages** (05 buyers by region×category; each book's
  participant build by entity range; declared per-key folds). Only if Stage III measured
  positive. Estimate −150…−300.
- **Stage VII — native + demolition.** Native only where a COLUMNAR core still profiles
  arithmetic-heavy; delete the object-participants layer, RegisterIndex remnants, the A-pool
  clone transport, pre-window shims. Rings only if they profile up.

**The honest landing bands (each with its §1.23 test):** I+IV+V serial ≈ 950–1,100; +II ≈
750–950; +III/VI if sharding pays ≈ 450–650; +VII ≈ 400–600. **No evidenced path reaches
100 ms/wk**: at bottom the week IS the register's ~110k rows, ~170k payments and ~2,500
firm-weeks, which §1.21 obliges the engine to compute. Sequence: I → II → (III ∥ IV) → V →
VI → VII. Declared-reorder registry: (1) Stage II dump-from-columns; (2) Stage III per-key
journal folds; (3) Stage VI per-key folds. Empty until each lands.

honest target" note.** That note was an argument about the CURRENT architecture (stage 05's

RELABEL judged by reference like any other. Current measured: 1,583 ms/wk (§7.300); stage 05
477, stage 08 409, ~40-stage tail ~700, GC ~8%.

W0 (measured, dead end): the existing clearing-worker pool parallelizes only the packed kernel

campaign's law: kill ceremony with columnar state end-to-end, shard the two big BODIES, and the

batch-converted → B5 objects demoted to views. Validation: SHARDS=1≡n, FP for pure steps,
references for the envelope relabel. 4 cores: 100 ms needs ~4-5× serial × ~3.5× workers — the
serial factor comes from B1/B5, the parallel from B2/B3/B4.

**THE LANGUAGE QUESTION, SETTLED BY SPIKE (2026-09-01):** the representative numeric core of a
company's week (income+TAXR+capex+learning, the transcendental-heavy mix) ported to Rust and

(call overhead + software libm; outputs bit-identical), against the real closure's 78,000 ns.

form is ported to C and integrated behind an injection point, piece by piece, oracle-validated:

   (NaN-propagating min/max, floor(x+0.5) rounds, uint32 mulberry32, toFixed via decimal
   round-trip) → **bit-compare every output lane** → integrate → STATE_DUMP differ at 4 and 13
   weeks, JS vs native, IDENTICAL required. A piece that cannot pass stays JS.

   build:native`, artifact never committed); the JS path REMAINS CANONICAL and always present

   `registerShardedKernel`; kill-switch `NATIVE_KERNELS=0`.

   all lanes bit-equal) → stage-05 settle/market cores (seam-extract first, §7.304's pattern) →

4. **Parallelism when it pays:** the box's cores are real (3 concurrent native kernels at ~97%
   efficiency, §7.308); the JS worker pool's SAB-mirror overhead is not — native threads arrive
   only after the serial native week is measured, never speculatively.

**Measurement-gated; profile first, always.** Two hard constraints: determinism is sacred
(same-seed A/B byte-identical), and no economic shortcut dressed as an optimisation. Owns the
damper's float half — and the small-cap equity tail that is HALF the §7.288 damper count. DIST

of arrays (an entity is an int32 row); strings die at the boundary (interning); relationships as
CSR; arenas, not allocation; payments as a columnar journal; a stage is a kernel over a range;
determinism by construction (row order, shard-order combines — the clearing engine is the proof

### DRV — the one derivative layer *(item 6b — BUILT §7.337; what remains, each at its gate)*

The layer is complete for the four classes it carries, and since §7.382 ONE STAGE runs them
(`stages/derivatives.ts`). What a NEW class needs: a profile module under
`domain/derivatives/classes/` (roles, PFE add-on, margin rate, ONE leg family — periodic or
mark — an event termination, a close-out), a member in `DerivativeClassId`, a line in
`DERIVATIVE_CLASSES` (compile-loud until named), and a MARKET module under
`stages/derivative-markets/` (its phase of the week, whether the standing book settles before or
after it, and the run that measures who needs it off their own books — through the standing
index, never a walk of the book — and strikes into the one book via `strikeDerivatives`), named
in `DERIVATIVE_MARKETS` (compile-loud until it is). The lifecycle, the merger re-key, the
close-out, the capacity charge and the margin path need nothing.
Remainders, each a registry flip or a named gate, none a redesign:
1. **Initial margin on the non-FX classes** is one number per profile (`initialMarginRate`, 0
   today for IRS/CDS/futures). Turning it on is a POLICY decision with real cash consequences
   (uncleared-margin rules; exchange IM on futures) — the user's call, not the campaign's
   (§1.21). The A side posts to the B side; the FX book's budget test is the template.
2. **The CDS PFE add-on by rating** — DONE (§7.341): `pfeAddOnRateFor(c, isInvestmentGrade)` on
   the CDS profile (5%/10%), the view carries `isInvestmentGrade(referenceId)`, the flat rate stays
   the capacity denominator (a desk sizes what it can write before it knows the name).
3. **A dealer between the IRS/CDS/futures sides** (07g's recorded "DER's next slice"): today
   hedgers meet end-users directly; a desk intermediary is a participant with the registry's
   capacity, no new machinery.
4. **An options CLASS** — premium is a periodic leg paid once, exercise is an event termination
   at intrinsic value: two profile methods. Its MARKET (who needs convexity, who writes it, the

5. **FX swap lines** stay gated on an FX funding market (§7.282's own gate).

### AU — Aurora, the UI rebuild *(item 8 — THE NEXT PROJECT; DESIGN BRIEF approved in principle 2026-09-01)*

**THE GOVERNING RULE (user, verbatim intent): "I want to be able to dig as deep as the world
allows."** Depth is the product requirement, not a feature. It binds as three obligations:
(1) **every object the engine names is addressable** — a firm, a fund, a region, a tranche, a
loan, a derivative contract, a supply contract, an invoice, an estate, an SME pool, a carrier
lane, a household cohort, a clearing book; (2) **every stored fact is viewable** — the floor
under every object is a typed field browser (`ALL`) that shows the whole record, nested objects
as links, so nothing the engine keeps is ever hidden by a screen's editorial choice; (3) **every
identifier rendered anywhere is a link** — a ticker in a holders table, a bank in a ladder, a
counterparty on a contract, a region on a curve: tap it and you are inside it. **No dead ends:**
a function with nothing to show says why (book empty, no history yet, mechanism armed and
inert), it never vanishes.

**THE METAPHOR: `OBJECT FUNCTION`** (Bloomberg's two nouns). An OBJECT is anything with an
identity; a FUNCTION is a way of looking at it; the command bar takes `ACME DES`, `usa 10y`,
`irs usa s5`, `oil 3m` and resolves them; everything on screen is itself an object. Rule 17 for
the UI: **an object type is one module, a function is one module** (`src/ui/objects/*`,
`src/ui/functions/*`), each function declaring `appliesTo` types; the shell and no screen ever
switches on a type outside the registries. Adding a screen or a new kind of thing is one entry.

**PHONE ONLY (user, 2026-09-01).** One full-screen panel; the command bar at the bottom with
autocomplete over the resolver; a back stack per panel; swipe between recently opened panels;
long-press an identifier opens it in a new panel. No desktop shell — the deployment target is
the installed PWA/TWA on the user's phone (§7.335), and the design is made for a thumb.

**THE VOCABULARY IS OURS.** The object/function metaphor is generic; a specific terminal's
command codes are its own. Functions are short typeable WORDS (a thumb types `chart`, not a
code), and the command bar reads `<object> <function>`: `krln overview`, `usa 10y chart`.
**THE FUNCTIONS (each a module; first set in build order):** `overview` · `all` — the typed
field browser, the depth floor · `chart` — history for any series (MoM/YoY per §1.9, level
where history is short) · `statements` — firm P&L/balance sheet/cash flow, a bank's sheet, a
region's national accounts · `holders` and `holdings`, both off the register · `peers` — the
screener (sortable, any columns) · `ladder` — debt and the maturity wall · `curves` —
sovereign, secured, swap spread, credit by rating, commodity, cross-currency basis · `book` —
a clearing book's float/demand/damper/print and the derivative book by class · `money` — the
payment trace by reason and category, the "1$ is 1$" screen · `links` — the relationship
graph (contracts, ownership, bank lines, hedge counterparties) · `macro` — a region's
dashboard · `diag` — the harness instruments live (damper by book, boundaries, identity
residuals, §6 watchlist) · `news` · `watch` — a grid of `object function` cells refreshed each
week · `bench` — the existing instrument, kept.

**TIME.** The world is turn-based: the shell owns Run / Step / Run N. A week scrubber lets
`GP` and `MON` look back. Where the engine keeps only a snapshot, the UI keeps its own TAPE
— a recorder logging the series being watched each week — so no engine state grows for the
UI's sake (§6.2's drift is real; §1.21 forbids the engine paying for a view).

**ARCHITECTURE.** Strictly read-only: `world` is `GameState` through typed selectors; the UI
never writes engine state (the determinism hash spans it — §3's rule-3 UI-state defect closes
here: `isTradeModalOpen`/`selectedInstrument`/`isNewsDrawerOpen`/game-over flags leave
`GameState`). Workspace state (panels, stacks, watchlists, tape) in a UI store persisted to
localStorage. React + Vite as today; the engine stays on the main thread as in the bench (a
week is ~1 s; the UI renders between weeks). The deferred `details`-bag discriminated union
(§5-STRUCT) lands here — the object registry is where instrument kinds get named. Inherits
§7.240's UI-rot inventory (every fix is "read the stored value"). Browser guards unchanged
(§7.333: bare `process` behind `typeof`, SAB behind availability checks).

**PARKED (user, 2026-09-01):** the game layer — player portfolio, trade ticket, dealers
(stage 12's legacy display-struct positions). Returns as `portfolio`/`trade` functions later.

**DESIGN CHOSEN (user, 2026-09-01):** the Aurora canvas, page "Slice 1"
(https://claude.ai/code/artifact/13a0ce35-936f-45c5-acf5-e163d6e54b78) — direction C's console
structure and palette (dark slate cards, segmented underline tabs, one accent used only for
links and the active tab), with a serif object name and A TOUCH of context: a word or two beside
a number only where it needs one (the covenant beside leverage, the statutory rate beside the
effective one). Statement-style financials: units line, indented items, ruled subtotals,

"1m Δ", "May 2028", "3 months of history", never "4w" or "w 71".
**SLICE 1 (approved):** resolver + phone shell + `overview`/`all`/`chart`/
`statements`/`holders`/`peers` over companies, institutions and regions; **slice 2 (§7.346): the

menu and sub menu, each view must adapt to what's showing, more functions and more objects, a

**REMAINDERS (each its own bounded commit):** `money` — the payment trace by reason and

function` cells refreshed each week (the tape already records what it would show); `curves`

cross-currency basis; `book` — the derivative book by class (today `contracts` on a region);
supply contracts and invoices as objects (the lane shows their totals; the rows are not yet
addressable); `estate` is reachable only once a firm has died (none in the first weeks — the

outside the repo because playwright-core resolves from there) and the screenshots read before

columns), and a screen that has nothing says why.

### CLOSE — the closed model *(item 10 — THE CAMPAIGN, user-ordered 2026-09-02)*

**THE RULE.** Money is created in two places and destroyed in their mirrors: a bank's credit (a
loan writes a deposit; repayment destroys both) and the central bank's purchases (reserves
against paper; redemption and QT destroy them). Everything else MOVES money between named
accounts. A quantity that appears or vanishes anywhere else is a defect with a size, and the
AUDIT prints it every week until it is zero. Nothing is netted away, nothing is clamped, and no
line is exempted "as a frontier": a counterparty this model does not have yet is a counterparty
to build, and until it is built the line fails the harness.

**THE AUDIT (`src/engine/audit/`, C1 — built §7.349).** Six families, one file each; a check is a
function; a finding is a week, a size and the identity that failed; the harness runs every check
every week, counts each finding as a violation, and prints the scoreboard at the end
(`=== THE AUDIT ===`: per check, weeks failed, worst size, last message; then "the money that is
not anyone's, last week"). **M money:** M1 the central bank closes exactly (assets = reserves +
TGA + currency, no unbacked line); M2 every named residual is zero (unbacked cash, the currency
plug, wholesale to nobody, tax from nobody, interest to nobody, household assets with no issuer,
income from nobody, boundary flows, unresolved money, the CCP residual); M3 the trial balance
(firms' cash by house bank = the corporate deposit lines; funds' = institutional; pools' = SME;
households' = the household line net of the pending T+1); M4 no negative balance anywhere; M5
every bank's sheet closes; M6 the money stock moves only by net lending and the central bank.
**O ownership:** O1 held = outstanding in BOTH directions per debt class and region; O2 shares
held ≤ issued and cap = price × shares; O3 register rows name live instruments and holders, none
on an acquired firm; O4 facilities on ladders = loans on banks, every loan a live borrower; O5
contracts have two live parties, estates recover ≤ owed, index weights sum to one, shipments
have live ends. **P prices:** P1 seniority orders one issuer's spreads (paper ≤ senior-secured
loan ≤ facility ≤ senior bond ≤ subordinated); P2 CDS basis bounded, recovery priced (40%) =
recovery delivered by closed estates; P3 spread ranks with rating and leverage, a defaulted firm
has no price; P4 no name bound eight weeks. **X cross-market:** X1 forwards non-negative, repo
inside the corridor, a solvent bank earns a margin, deposits pay below policy; X2 one good one
price across regions within a 2.5× wedge, futures within carry. **F accounts:** F1 a firm's
sheet closes and its filed cash is its balance; F2 the treasury's account moves by revenue −
outlays + borrowing + remittance; F3 world exports = world imports. **N names:** tickers and
names unique and well formed, ids `REGION_TICKER`, tranche ids carry the owner's ticker.
Remainders to add as the closures land: the journal's double entry by currency (needs the
`Money<C>` brand), CIP on the FX forward book, PPE roll-forwards, statement flows against the
journal by reason.

**THE SOURCES, NAMED BY THE BASELINE (13 weeks, §7.349) AND THE ORDER OF CLOSURE.**
(1) **The seed** — reserves are seeded as a GDP ratio PLUS every deposit class 1:1
(`initialization.ts`), while the central bank holds 15% of the sovereign stock: 245.6B of USA
bank money nothing was bought against at week 0 (463B across regions); and wholesale funding is
the seed's FUNDING RESIDUAL after deposits (`applyBankFundingSplit`) — 504B USA owed to a lender
that does not exist; and 10–35% of the sovereign stock is held by nobody (its coupon is paid to
nobody). C2 closes all three: the central bank's sovereign book = reserves + TGA − FX reserves
(the extra paper is real issuance to the central bank — QE at birth; debt/GDP rises and the
coupon comes back as remittance), household deposits are the funding residual (the seed's
unnamed lender IS the household sector), the sovereign stock is what the named holders hold,
currency is a stored liability at zero, `unbackedBankCashUSD` is deleted and M1 asserts the
identity to the dollar. (2) **The UNMODELED party** (C3): the wholesale roll at the funding
close and in 02b, the "seed supplier" key in 05, the book-settlement leftover guard. A bank
short at the close borrows from surplus banks (interbank unsecured — the lender's asset) and,
failing that, at the standing facility (the central bank's named loan); an unresolvable seller
key is the segment pool; a leftover is `defect()`. The kind leaves `PartyRef` (compile-loud).
(3) **The bank's second money engine** (C4 — DONE §7.352; what M1 still shows is the cross-border leg, C4b): `evolveBankingSector` moves cash by its own
arithmetic — interest on reserves from nobody, household loan interest and principal from
nobody (the household side is a budget deduction, not a debit), corporate deposit interest to
nobody, wholesale interest to nobody, dividends to nobody — and `bank-lending.ts` writes
reserves directly for consumer origination and household principal. Each becomes a payment
(CENTRAL_BANK → BANK; HOUSEHOLD → BANK; BANK → COMPANY pro rata; BANK → the register's holders;
BANK_CREDIT → HOUSEHOLD) and the evolution reads its cash from the ledger. `creditUnbacked`
(one site, PE proceeds) becomes the fund paying the founders. (4) **The treasury and the
households** (C5 — DONE §7.354): `governmentRevenueUSD` = the collections that arrived (the GDP × rate
formula credits 25.8B/wk to the account from nobody by week 13); coupons and redemptions only to
holders (everything is held after C2); the household residual income share (14.7% of income
"from nobody") deleted — capital income is what banks and issuers actually paid. (5) **Names**
(C6 — DONE §7.355). (6) **The price families, read as the closed world's own prices (§7.361):** the
facility is priced off the borrower's PD, the basis reads only cleared prints, and what the P family
then shows is ONE thing — the clearing books' weekly move caps BIND: 1,346 names bound 3+ weeks at
w13 (equity 824, half up and half down; corporate bonds 233, loans 172, paper 70 — every credit
name bound UP, a seller with no bid at any spread the cap allows). The damper is a recorded
derivation with a measured reason (§7.338 adapts it, §7.342 bounds the widening after an unbounded
one ran a book to 8,000T); the closed answer is not to delete it but to give every book its other
side — the desks' reservation and capacity as the bid of last resort at a price, the issuer's own
buyback and the float on the equity side — which is the next campaign (PRICES), not a line of this
one. P1/P2/P3 are re-read after it.

### WIRES — every asset moves by a numbered instruction, and everything clears by price *(item 11 — THE CAMPAIGN after CLOSE, user-ordered 2026-09-02)*

**The two principles (user, 2026-09-02):** (1) *everything clears by price only; the other quantities
are derived or calculated* — a yield, a spread, a discount margin, a coupon, a book's value are reads of
a price and the instrument's terms, never the thing a book clears on or a field a stage writes; (2)
*every asset move of any kind — money, a share, a bond, a tranche, a good, a house — carries its wire
number, or it does not compile.* The closure campaign (§5-CLOSE) did the second by hand for money;
this campaign makes it the data structure for every asset and then lets price be the only cleared
number. The five fixes of the same kind that follow from it (recorded 2026-09-02, all built on the
wire ledger): **D** derived quantities are functions, never fields (a stored sum of stored fields is a
compile error — the loan book and its loans, total debt and the ladder, market cap and price × shares,
the region's deposits and the banks' lines); **N** no pending state outside the journal (money in
transit, cash owed to holders, accrued coupons and taxes, the unfunded deficit are dated wires, not
side maps); **A** one kind of account for every party (settlement's nine-way switch becomes one
account store; the household sector and the pools are ordinary parties); **B** birth and death are
wires (a party leaves the world only by wiring every position to an estate and closing its account;
the O3/O4/O5 dead-party rows cannot exist); **R** a registry of every stated number with an owner and
a reason, enforced by a lint rule on numeric literals in the engine. Then **P** per-instrument price
clearing: the corporate books clear per tranche on price; the register keys rows by instrument in
face, valued at price × face; yield and spread are reads.

**The ledger.** `src/engine/ledger/wire.ts`: a columnar `WireJournal` (id, week, from, to, kind, asset,
quantity, price, reason, settle week), `wire(ctx, instruction)` returning the wire number — the one
write path for any asset. Money is the first asset kind (`MONEY`, unit USD, price 1): the payment
journal is settlement's projection of the week's money wires, and every `pay`/`payByIds`/
`journalPayment` row IS a wire. `state.nextWireId` persists the counter; the audit's W family reads
the journal. A wire is one instruction with both legs where an asset is bought (the asset one way,
the money the other, one number); the carrier is a holder while a good is in transit.

**The projects, in order (each its own commit, gated by the scoreboard):** W1 the wire ledger with
money (built §7.367). W2 securities (built §7.368): the holdings store's mutators sealed inside the ledger
(read-only views exported; a direct write is a compile error, a stage importing a mutator fails
hygiene), `transferHolding`/`issueHolding`/`retireHolding` the only writes — issue CREATES and retire
DESTROYS paper (the issuer's own book is never touched; an ETF is an issuer that is also a holder),
transfer moves it — the writers routed (fills through the clearing house, the desks' fills, the
primary's asset leg and the lead's residual, ETF creations/redemptions/baskets, mergers, estates,
the paying agent, sovereign redemptions); W2's audit line (the clearing house nets to zero per
region and kind) stays open until the non-register holders wire their fills — the banks' sovereign
investment books, the central bank's fills, the treasuries' bills, the households' equity — which
is W3's register-per-tranche work and the household register. W3 tranches (built §7.369): the ladder's
rows move by wires — `engine/ledger/tranche-ledger.ts` (`issueTranche`, `retireTranche`, `commitLadder`,
`rebuildLadder`, `moveFacilityLender`, `seedLadder`), the store sealed (`ReadonlyTrancheStore`, a row
carries the wire that created it, `pushLadderRow` demands one), a tranche's wire carrying its PAPER's
kind (bond, loan, commercial paper, bank facility) with the tranche id as the asset and face as the
quantity, market paper against the region's clearing house and a facility against its lending bank;
the register side of every corporate action settles through the house too, so the two sides of one
action meet there and the issuer's wires count once; W3's audit line (the ladders' change per region
and kind equals the issuers' net wires) is clean but for births (B's gap) and the estates of dead
firms (W6's). The register keyed per tranche is DEFERRED INTO P: while the books clear per issuer a
per-tranche register is pro-rata bookkeeping with no price behind it; P's per-instrument clearing
keys the fills by tranche by construction. W4 goods (built §7.370): every move of goods between two
parties a wire (kind GOOD, the sub-unit as the asset, units at the struck unit price) —
`engine/ledger/goods-ledger.ts` (`deliverGoods`, `receiveInputLot`, `settleOutputInventory`,
`setOutputStock`, `moveOutputUnits`, `scrapOutputUnits`, and the transformations `produceGoods` /
`consumeGoods` / `scrapGoods` that are not moves but the other half of the identity); the lot store
sealed (`ReadonlyLotStore`, `pushLot` demands the wire that delivered the lot), a firm's finished
stock written by the ledger only (hygiene); a NAMED carrier holds a consignment while it moves
(seller → carrier at dispatch, one consignment per carrier by the lane's shares, carrier → buyer
at arrival), a pool, a household and a treasury are sources and sinks; the kernels' FIFO draw
records what it consumed (a front lane); W4's audit line — per region and sub-unit, in units, the
stock's change equals production less consumption and scrappage plus the wires' net — prints no
line. W5 houses — VOID AS A WIRE PROJECT (decided 2026-09-02): the housing stock is a price-marked
aggregate (`housingStockUSD`, `homeEquityUSD`, a median price and an ownership rate); no dwelling
exists as a unit, nobody buys one from anybody, turnover is a rate and a mortgage is money — there
is no house to wire. A housing stock in units with owners is a BUILD project (a housing market that
clears dwellings by price between households, builders and estates), and its wires come with it;
until then W5 is not a gap in the ledger but a market that does not exist. W6 = B (built §7.371):
a private newborn's debt is a FACILITY its home bank lends it by wire (priced off its own default
probability at the bank's hurdle, booked on the bank as a loan, its proceeds funding the opening
balance ahead of the pool's carve-out — nothing appears on a ladder from nowhere; an FDI subsidiary
is born with no debt); a dead firm's estate retires the ladder face of every claim it recovers or
writes off (register claims through the clearing house, facilities against the bank) and a closed
estate extinguishes whatever face remained, so W3's line closes for births and deaths alike. Still
seeded without wires: the world's week-0 positions (the seed is the initial condition, not an
event) and a newborn's plant. **D (first slice built §7.372):** market cap and total debt are
READS — `marketCapOf` (price × shares, zero for a firm nobody can buy) and `totalDebtOf` (the
week-end ladder view) / `ladderTotalUSD` (the live rows, for a stage mid-week) — the two fields are
gone from `Company`, their eighteen writers with them, the company row store derives its two
columns at every fill (refresh, per-row sync, the field mesh, the checker — `DERIVED_F64_FIELDS`,
one derivation), the seed's cohort lift moves the SHARES and the ladder's face (the stored cap and
debt used to lift while their parts did not — O2's "market cap = price × shares" line existed
because a stored cap could disagree with its parts; the line is deleted as a tautology now), and
hygiene fails a `.marketCap` / `.totalDebt` read anywhere but the store and the kernel lanes. **Second slice (§7.373):** the bank's loan books
are reads of its rows (`loanBooksOf`), the regional aggregate has no loan book of its own
(`regionLoanBooksUSD` over the named banks), the seed's stated ratios survive only as the seed's
own sizing reads. **Third slice (§7.374):** an institution's total assets are a read of its cash,
receivables and rows (`institutionTotalAssetsUSD`); the week-end mark is deleted. D's one
remainder — the regional banking aggregate's other scalars, which 02b already rebuilds as the sum
of the named banks — falls to A (one account kind makes the aggregate a query). Then N, A, R as
their own passes, then P. **N (first slice built §7.376):** the journal's rows are dated; a pass settles what is due and carries the rest; corporate tax is the first obligation carried as dated wires. Remaining under N, in order: the households' T+1 hand-off, the unfunded deficit as the central bank's advance by wire, the holders' accrued coupons (with P). **A (first slice built §7.377):** the account store as a mirror with a gate (M7); A2 (§7.378) made it the authority of the pass and landed the household legs on the banks at once (N's money in transit closed); A3 persists the store, flips the readers and deletes the fields; A4 deletes the switch. **A3's shape (decided at handoff, §7.379):** per KIND, one commit each, companies' `cash` first — the store becomes PERSISTENT on the world for that kind (rows keyed by the party's KEY interned in v2, not its process-local id; `rowsOfParty` rebuilt on load), the mirror rebuild keeps feeding the other kinds' rows from their fields until their turn, the seed writes the store where it wrote the field, every reader takes `balanceOf` (`cashOf(v2, company)` for the 73 sites, a bank's COMPANY row being its reserves), and the field is deleted; then entities' `cashUSD`, the pools', the household sector's `depositsUSD`, the treasury's two (the unfunded deficit becoming the advance by wire — N's remainder); the bank lines last, because 02b's evolve and the bank-lending/transfer/estate rebuilds must become account operations first. The F1 line ("statement cash = ledger cash", ~46–63 firms every week) should close with A3.1 by construction — the statement will read the ledger.

**Gates.** Every project: `tsc`, lint, hygiene, the tests, and the harness scoreboard — M/O families
must not regain a line; the W family (W1 every wire has two live parties and a positive quantity; W2
the week's wires reproduce every holding's change) must print no line once its asset kind is wired.
The hygiene money-field rule is retired only when the type system enforces it.

defect is found, it becomes a step at its logical place. The scoreboard (§7.378's) is the standing
judge; every step names the line it closes.

### NEWS — a news system worth reading *(item 9 — SLICE 1 BUILT §7.346)*

Today `13-news` and `newsGenerator.ts` emit items; the ask is a news system that is SMART and
INTERESTING. The charter, in this repo's terms: **news is a derived narrative over events the
engine already records** — defaults and resolutions, births and FDI, M&A and divestitures, rating
moves, failed or under-subscribed auctions, credit events, mothballs and scraps, damper binds, a
bank's window at the CB, a contract break streak — each story CITING the state it derives from
(rule 1's cousin: a story with no traceable cause is decoration) and tracing the WHY through the
ledger ("KRLN missed its coupon because VOUL cut the line because the sovereign book repriced").
Stories rank by materiality to what the reader watches (AU's `watch`), follow up as the mechanism
plays out (a default story updates through the estate's workout), and every name in them is a
link (AU's depth rule). Rule 4 binds: no real-world narrative templates, no invented colour —
the interest comes from the causal chain being real. **Verify:** every population/credit/market
event yields a story; every story's claims resolve to state fields; a reader can walk any story
into the objects it names. **Built (§7.346):** defaults (with the ledger's why), rating crossings
and two-notch moves (the drift is a per-region digest), mergers, entries and builds abroad, plant
mothballed, a bank at the window, estates closed, each region's labour market (naming the firms
that moved it) and price level (naming the categories); `refs`, `kind`, `materialityUSD`,
`cause` on the item; the feed keeps a quarter. **Remaining:** follow-ups (a default story updating
through its estate), auctions failed/under-subscribed, damper binds and contract-break streaks
(the `diag`/`book` functions' material), ranking by the reader's `watch`.

### MGMT — corporate management in depth *(item 10 — SLICE 1 BUILT §7.344; the rest gated on measurement)*

Firms today decide through the company-week rules (capital programme, hiring, financing,
treasury, hedging) and one strategy branch (vertical integration). The ask is management with
DEPTH: a firm's management as a state that decides — capital-allocation policy (target leverage,
payout vs reinvestment), growth-versus-margin orientation, risk appetite, product-line entry and
exit, M&A intent, guidance — with **turnover**: a management that fails its own measured record
(sustained underperformance against peers, covenant breaches, a rating slide) is replaced, and
the replacement decides differently. Rule 19 is the constraint that shapes it: management
"styles" are SHAPE parameters unless they reduce to the two PREFERENCE primitives (patience and
risk aversion) plus measured circumstances — so the design is a per-firm preference pair plus
rules that read the firm's own books, never a stated style table. The game layer's return
(`portfolio`/`trade`) is the same seam: the player as one firm's management. **Verify:** outcome
dispersion traces to management choices; turnover follows measured failure and changes the
firm's path; no stated share or style anywhere.

### BRAINS — the preference primitives on every deciding entity *(user 2026-09-02; BUILT §7.344)*

**The user's diagnosis, confirmed:** every entity followed the same rules with the same constants
and maximised the same things, so every entity crossed every threshold in the same week —
crowding the channels, turning averages into cliffs. **The design, under rule 19:** the rules
stay uniform (that is physics); what differs is the two PREFERENCE primitives — patience (a
horizon in weeks, log-uniform on [a month, a year], median a quarter) and risk aversion (relative,
log-uniform on [0.5, 2], median 1) — drawn once per deciding entity from its own stream
(`domain/preferences.ts`), plus measured circumstances. **The median brain is yesterday's rule**,
so the population keeps the centre the uniform rules had and gains the dispersion that was
missing. Not smarter agents (uniform clever agents synchronise harder), not style tables, not
injected noise. Where each number enters, one site per threshold that was a constant:

- **Firms** — the labour affordability test runs on an adaptive EBITDA expectation at the
  management's horizon (the labour stage owns `expectedEbitdaUSD`), the required return is
  the premium × risk aversion, cut/hire speeds × / ÷ risk aversion; the capital programme's
  reinvestment envelope × (horizon / median), its stock-adjustment weight at 1/horizon, its rate
  drag, cash-health threshold and operating buffer × risk aversion; the payout ceiling ×
  (median / horizon); the CFO's action threshold, issuance pace and paydown pace through risk
  aversion; the hedge sized to risk-aversion-many sigmas; a buyer's reference price is the mean
  of the last `patience` prints (the §7.343 ratchet had every buyer on the same print), its
  cash-rationing threshold × risk aversion.
- **Banks** — the loan-pricing hurdle's premium × risk aversion; the cash buffer (wholesale
  raise, desk funding) × risk aversion.
- **Institutions** — the stated hurdle × risk aversion (the measured liability branches carry
  none); overweight multiple ÷ and full-size spread range × risk aversion.
- **Household cohorts** — the buffer target × risk aversion, the spend-down horizon ×
  (horizon / median); a cohort's brain is re-struck from its identity each rebuild.
- **Turnover (§5-MGMT slice 1)** — quarterly review; four failed quarters (expected margin
  below own baseline AND peers' median) replace the management with a new draw, with news.

**Verify:** the population's thresholds are dispersed (the test pins the draw's ranges, median
and determinism); no stated share or style anywhere; turnover follows measured failure. **Not
taken (each at its gate):** bank/institution patience (their expectation horizons — the PD a
bank prices, the drift a book chases — still shared constants); a firm's pricing/ask margin and
seller hurdle (price mechanism — measure first); the household tier equity share (a tier-level
shape, COH's); turnover for banks and boards of funds. **DIST-P:** +2 stated ranges, and the
constants they parameterise are now medians, not rules. **The clocks are the brain's too (§7.345):**
mothball after the management's horizon, scrap after four of them; production plans to the sales
the management expects at its horizon; the burn-in showed that shared clocks and shared
expectations were where the cliffs still were.

### DIST-P — the primitive scoreboard *(the rule is §1.19)*

The household cross-section needs TWO permanent primitives — PATIENCE and RISK AVERSION — plus one
temporary illiquidity friction; firms need zero to one. **§7.344 struck both, for every deciding
entity, as two log-uniform RANGES (`domain/preferences.ts`) — the only two stated shapes BRAINS
adds; every threshold they parameterise is now a median, not a rule.** **The count is the registry's (`domain/stated.ts`, §7.401 — eight declared; the ~41 shapes this section counted and the engine's ~1,380 fractional literals move into it area by area under the hygiene ratchet) and may never rise.** This model generates its own idiosyncratic income risk (a household is
unemployed because a real firm laid it off), which is the largest saving and already banked.
Open blocks: `TIER_OCCUPATION_MIXES` (dies with the occupation mix as an outcome of who firms
hire), `PARETO_ALPHA`/`NAMED_TIER_REVENUE_SHARE` (die with DYN), `WEALTH_SPENDDOWN_YEARS` (dies
when housing wealth is fully spendable).

---
## 6. Defects and watchlist

### 6.1 Live defects

**Emptied 2026-09-02 (user order): every live defect is a step of §5-FINALIZATION.** A defect
found from here on is added THERE as a numbered step at its logical place, never to a second
list. The rows this table carried — the unemployment ratchet, the dampers, behaviour in the
stages, the security display struct, unbranded money, the drifting level, the register's opening
size, the shock test at the damper, the institutional opening share, the manager/vehicle object,
FX swap lines, bank resolution's remainder, logistics scale, the UK/EUR bank watches, the last
boundary line's successor (the interbank market), the consequence rows, and the scoreboard's
standing lines — are all in it; §7 keeps their history.

### 6.2 Watchlist — measure, do not fix

| Metric | Why |
|---|---|
| The money family (M1–M7) and "the money that is not anyone's" | §5-CLOSE deleted every named gap; since §7.364 the family is CLEAN every run and the unowned line reads 0.00B. Two lessons to keep: an audit can print a leak that is its own (§7.373: the snapshot omitted a deposit class — one `depositsOf`); a unit threshold in the engine's shard merge dropped shares as if they were dollars (§7.373). The scoreboard is the watch; a line here is a defect at its site. |
| The TGA over a quarter | Sawtooths on remittance dates — watch the LEVEL, not the shape. |
| Occupational mismatch | Produced by sector composition moving faster than retraining. |
| Sovereign price elasticity to a size-only bidder | The books ARE thin (consistent with the damper row). |
| Household income top-down vs bottom-up | `estimatedHouseholdIncomeUSD` remains the top-down anchor. |
| The named private tier still sells nothing | ~300 seeded private firms/region carry `productLines: []`. |
| Loan-book Spearman noise | 0.26–0.76 at 23–32 names; re-measure as the universe grows. |
| The two credit-ETF w3 dust singles | 0.01B, baseline since §7.275; likely in-kind slice edge or pending-settlement timing at the bound (§7.262). |
| **THE 1e-8 WEEK-1 DRIFT (§7.370)** | Introduced with the goods ledger: three firms differ at the eighth digit at week 1 (two industrials' input cost, one bank's reserves), amplified by the chaotic dynamics to a 13% price-index gap by week 13 with unemployment and GDP unchanged. Not the split, the wires, the records, the interning order or the arithmetic (all ruled out, §7.370). Owner: whoever next runs a byte-gate on stage 05's stock settlement / the estate's inventory path — a bisection by file, ONE dump per step. Watch it DOWN to zero, never widen a tolerance for it. |
| **THE STATE-GROWTH DRIFT (on-device, §7.335)** | Weekly cost climbs ~45% weeks 5→80 (~700–750 → ~1,030–1,140 ms) on two independent 85-week phone runs tracking the SAME curve — thermals excluded, all stages inflate roughly proportionally (shares constant). First suspect: the contract book's measured growth (§7.328: 20,124 → 32,705 rows, ~3k/wk) and its ∝-rows settle cost; roster and history growth behind it. Measure before fixing; whoever resumes §4.C or profiles long runs owns the read. |

### 6.3 The clamp discipline

**A clamp is almost always covering for a decision that is not being taken.** Delete it only once
the decision is real (§7.167), and never delete a stand-in before the mechanism it stands in for
exists (§7.158, §7.177). The programme is closed (§7.176); the rule survives.
