Do not stop at stage 2, and do not roll it back for the interim print (§1.20's spirit): the mirror is what makes the writer flip gateable at all.
315. **THE HOLDINGS WRITER FLIP LANDED, FAILED ITS FIRST TIMING TEST, WAS FIXED WHERE THE PROFILE POINTED, AND SETTLES AT COST-NEUTRAL — the campaign's most instructive milestone.** The flip itself (rows authoritative; the clearing store pairs objects to row ids at build and RELINKS chains at write-back so only fills pay an intern; every post-window writer row-native — corporate actions, sovereign redemption, bill accretion, the ETF legs, merger mint/re-key, estate seizures by row-id index, consolidation on integer pair keys; `itemizedHoldings` a week-end materialized view; per-write syncs all deleted) gated clean: 4wk/13wk logs IDENTICAL, state canonically identical — the gates caught one real drift on the way, the household portfolio's post-window object reads (equity attribution + ETF NAV) seeing the stale week-start view. **Then the battery measured +83 ms/wk (+5.8%) against the pre-mirror tree — the flip did NOT collect §7.314's toll.** The per-stage profile named two causes, neither guessed: the write-back's Set-diff relink (2.8 → 25.1 ms — hashing ~110k row ids weekly), and ~40–50 ms of UNTIMED week-end materialization (~110k fresh view objects; the tranche flip pays the identical pattern on 8k rows, invisibly). Fixes, each mechanical once named: an epoch stamp on a typed mark column replaces every relink Set (write-back 25.1 → 13.4 measured), and materialization is DIRTY-TRACKED — chain mutations mark through the store helpers, in-place column writers mark explicitly, core rebuilds only marked books; a clean book's last-close array carries forward (nothing mutates view arrays any more, so it aliases nothing), and a missed mark cannot survive gating because HOLDINGS_SYNC_CHECK compares EVERY book to its rows. Re-gated identical at 4/13wk. **Final battery (20wk, medians of 3, interleaved): pre 1,479.6 → post 1,487.4 ms/wk, +0.52% — inside the noise floor, ranges overlapping: COST-NEUTRAL.** What the milestone bought at zero speed cost: the register is row-authoritative (unblocking column-read clearing adapters, native ports, and workers over the register), two silently-unsynced ETF writers and the household stale-read class are found and dead, and THE LESSON, measured twice now: **in this codebase an object structure's cost is its ALLOCATION (views, rebuilds, spreads), not its traversal — a flip that deletes traversal but adds materialization can lose, so views materialize dirty-only or not at all.** Course correction, profile-ordered (12wk HEAD profile): stage 05 is 381 ms (26.7%), stage 08 is 280 ms (19.7%), the clearing adapters ~240 ms combined; the price-history rings measured out of the top ten (≤12-element region/category series) and are DEPRIORITIZED by that measurement — §5-SCALE's next slices are 05's fattest S05_PROF phase, an S08_PROF split of 08, then the adapters' schedule builders on rows.
316. **THE ENGINE IS UNIFORMLY WARM — the profile campaign's instruments (S05B/S08/S08K, all free when off) decompose every remaining big stage into 25–60 ms terms with NO hot spot anywhere.** Stage 05's buyers walk: lot legs ~35, invoice+fx ~43, aggregate-payers ~28, plan overhead ~40. Stage 08: front+indexes ~78, back kernel ~150, corp-actions ~30, accruals ~30–55 (after trimming `buildFromRows` to its consumed projection — the by-instrument transpose, shares, region and row-in-holder columns had NO consumer and cost ~110k Map ops a week; `rowsOfInstrument` now throws on the skipped index rather than answering empty). The back kernel itself: capital ~30–58, cash walk ~30, debt ~40–53, tail ~30–46. First grind slices landed differ-identical (lane-share hoists to per-(origin,region), seller-pid once per lot, zero-region skip in aggregate-payers). **What this means, stated for the record: serial grinding from here buys single digits per slice — the step change is the PARALLEL path.** Fresh pool probe on this tree: `FRONT_WORKERS=3` runs EQUAL to serial after its 12 s first-week spin-up — §7.308's ×0.76 loss is gone — but the front pool shards only ~75 ms of work, invisible in the noise floor. The back kernel (~150 ms) is ALREADY shard-isolated (own accumulators, own journal, ordered fold, order-invariant per §7.222) and stays inline for exactly one reason: company OBJECTS cannot cross the SAB boundary. **The next rock is therefore the back half's seam→core→post conversion (the §7.306 step-12 treatment): typed lanes in, pure numeric core over rows, object write-back — which unlocks pooling both kernel halves (~230 ms shardable) and the back core's native port, with the clearing books' per-market sharding (~240 ms, per-shard journals folded in market order — 08's own proven pattern) behind it.** The price-history rings stay deprioritized (§7.315).
317. **THE BACK-HALF SEAM, SCOPED BEFORE BUILT — three facts that change the plan's order, each measured or read off the tree, none guessed.** (1) The entire back kernel makes ONE RNG draw (the rating-change smoothing, stage08-back ~line 947) — so its capital block and cash walk are draw-free and can move to all-firms passes with NO capture/resume machinery: the remaining closure resumes `F.rngAfter[row]` untouched and its one draw sees the same value, bit-exact by construction. (2) The profile dispatch (banks/insurers/managers/carriers, ~75 firms) reads ctx/state/entityById DEEPLY — other entities' live books — and stays on objects until those structures port. (3) THE ONE THAT REORDERS THE QUEUE: re-looping the kernel's blocks into passes buys ZERO serial time — the pass would read the same `comp.*` objects per firm, just in a different loop; §7.316's flat profile is the cost of the OBJECT REPRESENTATION, and a pass split pays only when its inputs are typed lanes (native/worker-transportable). **The prerequisite for pooling the ~460 ms of shardable week is therefore THE COMPANY ROW STORE — `state.companies` as persistent typed columns on v2 (the tranche/holdings staging: mirror + check, readers file-by-file, writers, week-end materialization), ~100 field lanes read by the back kernel alone.** That is the campaign's next arc, and it is the LAST big flip: after it, both kernel halves and the clearing adapters are pure row cores, which is what workers and the native ports have been waiting for. Sequencing within it, §7.311's proven order: the hot scalar lanes first (cash, revenue, PP&E, debt aggregates — the back kernel's read set), the nested structures (productLines, construction, inventories) by sub-store as lots/tranches/holdings were, the UI/dump view materialized dirty-only (§7.315's lesson, applied from day one this time).
318. **§7.317 SLICES 1–3 LANDED BYTE-IDENTICAL, AND THE WORKER-BLOCKER AUDIT IS COMPLETE — the back kernel's remaining shared-mutable surface is four named items.** Landed, each 4wk/13wk BYTE-identical: (1) the capital block as one pure-call unit (`runCapitalBlock`, twelve outputs plus the maintenance credit event returned as data and emitted at its original sequence point); (2) the cash walk as one pure-call unit behind `makeCashPoster` (one mutable cash box, one ledger, one post — the walk and every later block write through the same instance, so the journal is untouched; the §7.316 contended money-fund redemption deliberately stays outside, in firm order); (3) the holder-register channels shard-isolated like the tax maps (accruals/cash by per-key sums, the payout set by ordered union, the corporate-action ratios by per-key PRODUCTS — their compose rule — merged in shard order = firm order). **What still pins the kernel to the main thread, exhaustively:** (A) the company OBJECTS themselves — the seam→core→post conversion, the giant; (B) the tranche store's in-loop writes (pushLadderRow/relink) — needs the SAB mirror + per-shard-sink treatment the LOT table already proved (§7.306 step 13); (C) the two contended resources (mmf sweep + lead-bank award, plus the sweep's region-fund lookup) — move into the combine, drawn in row order; (D) the ~75 profile firms' deep object reads (entityById/state) — pin them to the main-thread shard. entityById and the carrier/channel revenue maps are READ-frozen during the loop and lane-able at the seam. B, C and D are bounded, proven-pattern work; A is the arc.
319. **PHASE-1 LANING LANDED THROUGH STEP 1.6b — five byte-identical commits — AND THE SEAM RESIDUE IS NOW AN EXACT LIST, not an estimate.** Landed (each 4wk/13wk BYTE-identical, branch+main): the BackLanes seam (typed lanes with NaN-as-undefined replaying every `??`/`=== undefined` exactly; the three productLine×region folds reduced at the seam in inline fold order); the capital core object-free (comp writes returned as data, applied at the original write points); the cash walk object-free (resolved scalars + identity strings; the poster stays the main-side emission channel); the debt/tail SCALARS on lanes (33 sites: shares, price, yields, eps, cds, beta, baselines, the earnings clock, the offering cooldown, default/merger/listing flags, rating/name); identity strings on lanes (76 sites). **The closure's remaining comp reads, counted: 80.** Their classes, each with its disposition: (a) ARRAYS/OBJECTS — dealerConsensus, historicalFundamentals/Prices, revenue/ratingHistory, bankBalanceSheet, treasuryHoldings, wipBySubUnit, productLines(1), occupationMixDrift(1) — these need CSR/side-array lanes (bounded rings, mostly ≤8 entries) and are the seam's remaining REAL work; (b) WRITE-BACK-ENTANGLED prior-value reads (previousCapex/employeeCount-style RHS snapshots) — laning one changes WHICH value is read unless per-site assignment order is proven; convert only with per-site analysis, never by sweep; (c) mmfSharesUSD — mutated mid-loop by the liquidity redemption, inseparable from the §7.318(C) contended design; (d) the profile branch and the vehicle test — main-side by §7.318(D). The write-back's 81 assignments are the post pass (step 1.7), lifted LAST. Phase 2.1 (the tranche SAB mirror) proceeds in parallel with (a).
320. **PHASE-1 LANING COMPLETE THROUGH 1.6d — the shard-core span reads no company object except its four recorded exceptions — and the differ caught the SEAM-VALIDITY trap on its first try.** Steps 1.6c/d landed (both re-gated BYTE-identical at 4/13wk): the last safe scalar reads (23 sites, each POSITION-VERIFIED to read the pre-write value before converting — the write-back's prior-value snapshots make a blind sweep unsound), then accruedTax/bankCapitalRatio/concentrations/vehicle-flag/trace-key/news-names (the cash walk takes `hasVehicle` as a flag; its posts never read the entity). **THE TRAP, for every future seam: a lane is valid only if NOTHING between the seam and the read mutates its source.** revenueVolatility looked seam-computable and is not — the PROFILE modules append `comp.revenueHistory` MID-LOOP (profiles/bank.ts:52 and siblings), after any seam and before the rating reads it; the seam-side fold rated profile firms on last week's history, week-2 ratings moved a notch UP across the profile cohort, and the differ named them in one bisection. The fold stays closure-side with the reason stated in stage08-lanes.ts; the profile-write audit then cleared every other lane (the only profile-written laned field was revenueHistory itself). **The core boundary now stands: capital→cash→debt→rating reads lanes + v2 rows only, except (i) the profile branch (§7.318 D, main-side), (ii) mmfSharesUSD (§7.318 C, contended), (iii) the capital-write application lines (the future lane writes), (iv) the revenueHistory fold (the mid-loop-append constraint above). The earnings/filing/write-back zone (~line 1655 on) is the main-side POST.** Next: the core lift (the span as one function over its measured local-variable interface), then Phase 2's mirror and combine, then the pool.
321. **PHASE 1 COMPLETE — THE BACK KERNEL IS SEAM→CORE→POST — and the §7.318(C) contention has an EXACT design, not a workaround.** `runBackCore` (capital → cash walk → liquidity → debt → rating) lifted verbatim over its MEASURED crossing interface (50 values + the cash poster + the creditMetrics pair the declaration regex missed and tsc caught); the closure keeps the inactive guard, the core call, and the main-side POST (earnings, filing, buyback, sweep, write-back), re-deriving reg/weekUpdate/TS by the same lookups. Gates BYTE-identical at 4/13wk throughout; ~10 commits on branch+main. **The contended-resource design (Phase 2.2), from reading the actual draw sites:** (1) the treasury SWEEP is in the POST — main-side row order already, NOT a worker problem; only the LIQUIDITY REDEMPTION (mid-core, feeds the same firm's default decision) contends. (2) The exact-semantics resolution is a BARRIER SPLIT: core-A (capital + cash walk — no contention, no draws) runs parallel; the redemptions execute ON MAIN in row order (2,492 cheap probes against the regional books — precisely the first-come order the inline loop had); core-B (liquidity decision + debt + rating — the kernel's one RNG draw, already per-firm-stream) resumes parallel with the corrected cash. Two barriers per week, zero semantic change. (3) The LEAD-BANK award (mid-B, desk capacity first-come) DEFERS: the core computes the offering minus its lead; the combine assigns leads and enqueues in row order — nothing downstream in the same firm's core reads the ticker, so deferral is exact. What remains for the pool: the A/B split of runBackCore (the proven lift technique, one more crossing interface), the tranche SAB mirror (§7.306's lot pattern), profile rows pinned main-side, and the poster's worker variant emitting interned-id journal pushes.
322. **THE BARRIER SPLIT LANDED BYTE-IDENTICAL — AND THE EXACTNESS AUDIT FOUND THE KERNEL'S TRUE SERIAL CHAIN: the money-fund book itself.** `runBackCore` now composes `runBackCoreA` (capital + cash walk — verified: no draws, no contention, no running-net reads, no cross-firm live reads) → `runMmfRedemption` (the §7.321 barrier) → `runBackCoreB` (liquidity + debt + rating; redeclares and returns the four income values it mutates), sequentially, so serial behavior is unchanged (4wk/13wk BYTE-identical). Verified for the reorder: `pendingNetById` is WRITTEN but never READ inside the kernel, and `committedLineHeadroomUSD` is pure own-firm arithmetic. **The blocker the audit then found: the POST's treasury sweep REFILLS the redeemable pool (`book.redeemableUSD += sweepUSD`, money-market-fund.ts:170) that the CORE's redemptions draw — so red(k+1) legitimately sees sweeps ≤k, and the dependency chain red(k)→B(k)→post(k)/sweep(k)→red(k+1) serializes B through the book.** Exact options, decided: (i) POOL A NOW (A depends on nothing contended; the chain starts at the redemption) with the middle kept serial — the first pool step, exactness-preserving; (ii) full B-pooling requires EITHER carrying the book chain through a serial middle (kills the win) OR the DECLARED CHANGE of crediting sweep-ins to the pool at week-end (T+1 — arguably MORE realistic: a corporate sweep deposit is not intraday liquidity to other redeemers) — that is an economics call above this campaign's writ, put to the user with measurements when the A-pool number exists, never decided silently (§1.12/§1.20). What the A-pool still needs, enumerated for the build: weekUpdate scalar lanes (~8: sales/purchases/receivables/payables/capex), the typed worker poster (pre-interned pids: self, unmodeled, household/government per region, home bank, the per-region channel-holder lists), per-shard typed holder-accrual buffers (interned `type:issuer` keys), and the profile rows pinned main-side.
323. **THE T+1 SWEEP CREDIT — §7.322(ii), USER-AUTHORIZED, LANDED, AND MEASURED LATENT: byte-identical at 4 and 13 weeks.** A corporate sweep-in still issues fund shares and settles as cash, but no longer refills the intraday redeemable pool (`book.redeemableUSD += sweepUSD` deleted, reason stated at the site): the pool is the fund's OPENING cash, drawn by redemptions only, and sweep money joins at next week's open — the T+1 behavior a real corporate sweep deposit has. Measured: 4wk and 13wk dumps BYTE-IDENTICAL to the pre-change tree, logs identical — the refill has never mattered at these horizons (no redemption ever drew on an intraday refill), so today the change is LATENT; what it buys is structural: **the back kernel's redemption barrier no longer depends on the post phase, so the §7.321 phase order (all-A parallel → redemptions in row order → all-B parallel → posts in row order) is exact by construction.** The residual ordering nuance, stated for the record: core-phase redemptions now all precede post-phase sweep-redemptions in the pool's draw order (was per-firm interleaved); with the pool never binding this is equally latent, and it becomes observable only in a run where a regional fund's opening cash is exhausted — a run the §6 watchlist would flag on its own.
324. **BACK_BARRIER=1 IS BYTE-IDENTICAL AT 4 AND 13 WEEKS — the pool's execution order is now a verified serial oracle, and the road there caught two float-tree traps the differ named exactly.** The mode runs the §7.321 phase order on main — all core-A in row order (each under the firm's front stream: the PROFILE branch draws in A, so B resumes from the stream A leaves, captured per firm), all redemptions, all core-B, all posts — with every phase's emissions captured PER FIRM and folded back as [A, red, B, post] per firm in row order. Exactness needed two mechanisms beyond the § 7.322 audit: (1) **the running settlement net**: `addPending` applies at EMISSION time, so phase order changed per-party float summation trees; barrier mode defers it (`ctx.deferPendingNet`) and the merge applies each journal leg's net in the merged (= original) order (`applyPendingLeg`). (2) **the sweep books' `netInflowUSD`**: a shared running sum whose phase-ordered accumulation drifted mmfSharesUSD at the last ULP — 30 diffs at week 4, CASCADING to 53 across 5 families by week 13 (an ULP seed is a divergent trajectory, not a bounded relabel — measured, and the reason "declare the new tree" was rejected). Fix: the true deltas are plumbed out exactly (`runMmfRedemption` returns paidUSD; the post sweep reports `shareDeltaUSD` via `onSweepDelta`), and the barrier rebuilds each book's netInflow from zero in interleaved firm order. Normal mode stays byte-identical throughout; the flag costs nothing off. **What the pool now needs is transport only: run the A loop's and B loop's bodies on workers (the phase semantics are proven), with the typed poster, the tranche SAB mirror for B, and profile rows pinned main-side.**
325. **THE BARRIER'S CAPTURE MECHANISM REBUILT AT PHASE GRAIN — overhead ~1,040 → ~10 ms/wk, byte-identical — and the measurement that forced it is the transport's design law.** Measured first (S08K_PROF, 20wk steady state): serial kernel ~110 ms/wk (capital ~12, cash ~19, debt ~24, tail ~30); §7.324's barrier ~1,150 ms/wk — the per-firm capture allocated ~12 objects (a fresh PaymentJournal among them) per firm per phase (~10,000 allocations/wk) and merged Maps per firm, TEN TIMES the kernel it was capturing. **The rebuild: ONE accumulator set per phase, per-firm END-OFFSET marks into every family (arrays by length, the journal by n, the Maps/Set by size — sliceable because every kernel holder key carries the firm's own companyId, one writer per key, and Maps preserve insertion order), and one firm-major replay appending exactly the captured entries in [A, red, B, post] order per firm** (journal legs via journalPush + applyPendingLeg in merged order; §7.324's netInflow rebuild unchanged). The one family that is NOT sliceable — the tax maps, many firms adding into one region key — is captured EXACTLY: the cash walk reports its two written amounts per row through `d.taxCapture` (NaN = no write, so a genuine zero write still creates its key) and the replay adds those same floats in firm order; a delta recovered by subtracting map values would be a different float (§7.324's lesson, applied in advance this time). Gates: BACK_BARRIER=1 byte-identical at 4/13wk, normal mode byte-identical, tsc/lint/hygiene/tests clean. **Barrier-mode kernel now ~106–133 vs serial ~96–129 ms/wk. What this settles for the pool: the main-side half of the worker transport (capture, ordering, replay) is built and effectively free; workers only need to produce the same per-firm segments the phase loops produce today. And the design law, measured twice now (§7.315, here): capture is offsets into shared flat structures, never per-unit objects.**
326. **THE A-POOL LANDED BYTE-IDENTICAL ON ITS FIRST RUN — the first workers ever inside the back kernel — and the battery prices it honestly: COST-NEUTRAL, with the win gated on B riding the same worker.** Built (`engine2/back-pool.ts`/`back-worker.ts` on the front pool's skeleton, BACK_WORKERS=n opt-in, default off; BACK_WORKERS implies the barrier path): workers run the SAME `runBackCoreA` (comp: null — W1's form) for every active non-profile firm while the main thread runs the ~75 profile firms' A; worker emissions come back as §7.325-shaped segments (typed journal + per-firm end offsets, holder entries, credit events, exact tax columns) and fold into the same firm-major replay; a dead pool re-runs its firms serially under the same capture — the world is identical on every path, and a debug env (CASH_LEDGER/LEARN_TRACE/BYPASS_TRACE/BOUNDARY_TRACE) declines the pool. **The identity discipline that removed all id remapping:** the main thread PRE-INTERNS, per firm in row order, every party core-A can emit (self, unmodeled, household/government of the region, home bank + its credit, the vehicle institution) and the walk's settled reason labels; each worker's intern tables are seeded with the full table deltas in id order, so every id a worker writes is canonical — and a worker whose tables grow anyway THROWS, killing the pool for the run (an enumeration miss costs speed, never correctness). **Gates: BACK_WORKERS=3 byte-identical at 4 AND 13 weeks, logs identical but the engagement line; tests/tsc/lint/hygiene clean. Battery (20wk × 3 each, interleaved, steady-state weeks 4–20): serial 1,394 vs pool 1,411 ms/wk — ranges overlapping, COST-NEUTRAL — plus a one-time ~20 s worker spin-up (3 × tsx compiling the engine graph, week 1 only).** The overhead split, measured (S08_PROF [s08w]): dispatch ~7 ms (lane clone-out), profile-A ~2–3, collect ~0 — the workers finish INSIDE the main thread's own A window, so the parallel slack is real — and **rebuild ~26–43 ms: re-materializing the 47-field A crossing per firm (structured-clone objects arrive dictionary-moded and tax every later B/post read) costs more than the ~31 ms of A it offloads.** That is §7.315's allocation lesson wearing a new coat: the crossing-as-plain-object interface is the cost, not the compute. **The completion path, stated for the record: B runs on the SAME worker (the crossing never ships — only B's thin outputs do, and B's ~24 ms joins the offload), which needs the tranche SAB mirror, offerings/news as journaled data, the revenueHistory post-A lane, and the lead-bank deferral (§7.321(3)) — exactly the journaling rails Phase 4/5 build for the clearing family (~240 ms: 07b 64 + 07d 62 + 07e 62 + 07f 52, re-measured this session) and stage 05. The campaign proceeds to Phase 4 with the pool's machinery proven and waiting.**
327. **PHASE 4'S FIRST FIVE SLICES: −89 ms/wk MEASURED (1,394 → 1,305 median, ranges non-overlapping, −6.4%) — the campaign's largest battery-verified gain since the writer flip, from killing per-pair map probes and per-unit allocations the §7.300 profile named exactly.** The line-level profile (esbuild bundle + --cpu-prof, 12wk, re-run at HEAD to confirm each fix moved its term): (1) **07e's demand build on dense columns** — the entities × companies pair loops carried ~6 string-Map probes and two `positionKey` string builds per pair; per-company values now sit in arrays indexed by the region's company index, the lent/buy-in books re-group by entity once, `entityRequiredReturn` hoists per entity (it was called per PAIR); 07e measured 61.8 → 37.1 ms/wk (−40%), the term gone from the profile's top 30. (2) **The corporate-action settlement's two full register walks fused** — holders-of-record totals and the touched-entity pre-scan chased the same ~110k-row chains separately every week; one pass computes both in the same entity/chain order. (3) **consolidateRegister's dup-scan on a persistent epoch-stamped key map** — the per-book `Set` (~3k allocations/wk, every row hashed in) replaced by §7.315's mark pattern at (type, instrument) grain; the term left the top 30. (4) **accumulateShard's per-fill string probes hoisted** — instrument ids and per-participant holdings maps resolved once per book; the net-cash roundtrip runs on a dense per-participant array flushed after the shards (the map is pre-populated per participant, so insertion order and float sequence are untouched — the §7.324-class check that makes such a flush legal). (5) Accrual-pass micro-hoists. **Every slice byte-identical at 4 AND 13 weeks; tests/lint/hygiene clean; family total ~240 → ~202 ms/wk; cumulative vs session start 1,558 → 1,305 (−16.2%).** What the re-profile says is next, in measured order: stage 05's buyers walk and settleContracts (~25 ms/wk each — the invoice-book columnar store, Phase 5), the front pass + seam (~60 ms/wk combined — §7.317's company-row-store arc), the ClearingResult int-indexed interface flip (~19 ms/wk in accumulateShard's remaining map writes plus every adapter's string-keyed apply pass, a §7.311-style staged conversion), and the corp-action walk's remaining ~17 ms/wk.
328. **THE §1.23 KILL TEST FIRED ON ITS FIRST TRACK — the stage-05 micro grind is STOPPED, by its own rule, after two byte-identical slices that measured ~nothing.** Slice 2 (settle's customers slotted, need per customer slot, lazy per-slot updateOf/pidOf handles): settle eff ~50 → ~45, under half the named term. Slices 3–4 (buyers-walk ex-works/arrival matrices per market, buyerUpdate hoisted, per-lane carrier arrays with pid/region resolved at first-use order): buyers ~130–147, UNCHANGED. Both gated byte-identical at 4/13wk and kept (clean, harmless). What the failures MEASURE: stage 05's walks are already probe-lean — their cost is the per-row emission floor (payByIds legs, invoice booking, inventory pushes over ~27k lots and a contract book measured GROWING 20,124 → 32,705 rows across weeks 7–12, ~3k/wk, settle eff ∝ rows at ~2 µs/row) — so the remaining stage-05 wins are representation changes (§4.C IV.1/IV.2: the invoice and contract stores, capping touches), not cheaper probes. Effort reorders per the rule to the evidenced items: the ClearingResult int-indexed flip (~19 ms, §7.327-named), then STAGE II — the company row store, the spine.
329. **STAGE II.1–II.4a LANDED — the company row store exists, and it runs on TRUST: writer-synced all week, no weekly rebuild — the spine's foundation, structurally neutral so far by design.** Landed byte-identical at 4/13wk throughout: (II.1) ~108 scalar lanes (85 numeric NaN-sentinel / 5 flags / 18 strings) on SharedArrayBuffer with copy-on-grow, `buildBackLanes` ALIASING its 34 numeric + 7 string 1:1 columns zero-copy; (II.3a) the front seam's 26-field scalar block reading lanes with the `??`/`||` resolutions replayed exactly; (defect, found by profile) the store was KEYED BY GameState, which stage 13 REBUILDS weekly (`{...state}`) — every week re-allocated the whole ~3 MB SAB store, ~11.5 ms/wk of pure allocation; re-keyed by the persistent V2World; (II.4a) THE TRUST FLIP: a one-run writer census (`COMPANY_STORE_AUDIT` — per-stage staleness first-sights after every stage) named every company-field writer in the pipeline, each now carries a field-targeted lane resync postlude in core.ts (01 cash; 02b bankMarketShare/totalDebt; 07b oas; 07e price/mcap; 07f totalDebt; 07h cds pair; 09 concentrations; 10-mergers thirteen fields; hc-lifecycle five; securities-lending; money-fund-income; settlement ×2; estate-resolution), stage 08's write-back dual-writes each firm's whole row, births fill on arrival — and the weekly refresh is DELETED. `COMPANY_SYNC_CHECK` clean over 13 weeks. **Timing pulse: 1,366 ms/wk single-run vs the 1,305 battery median — within single-run noise, i.e. the arc is cost-neutral to date, exactly as §4.C priced the scaffolding stages (§7.314's precedent: the payback events are the copy deletions — the seam's nested walks onto sub-stores (II.2), downstream reader flips (II.3), and objects-to-views (II.5) — not the store's existence).** What the store already changed: lanes are valid at EVERY stage boundary, so any stage's company reads can flip to rows now, and the §7.320 seam-validity trap cannot exist for laned scalars (one representation, mesh-synced).
330. **II.5's PILOT FIELD IS DONE: `revenueHistory` is COLUMNAR — the first Company field deleted from the object, by the method the rest of Stage II now follows.** The 13-slot ring on V2World (plain arrays, not SAB — batteries deep-clone v2, and SAB lanes would SHARE across clones: a noted §1.24 deviation, workers get mirrors per §7.306) encodes unset-vs-explicitly-empty because the seed writes both and `||` distinguishes them; the push replays `[...(hist || [v]).slice(-12), v]` exactly, absent→[v,v] included. All writers (front post, the four profiles, the merger spin, three seed sites) and readers (two rating folds, contract terms, labor demand via a reused scratch, the PD vol) converted; the §7.320 mid-loop-append trap is structurally dissolved — profiles push to the ring, the fold reads the ring. **THE METHOD, proven here: delete the field from the interface → tsc names every typed site → grep names the cast-hidden ones → the per-week A/B differ (§1.22) verifies behavior.** And its first catch: the carriers bootstrap seeded the field inside an `as unknown as Company` literal tsc could not see; carriers started with the wrong history length, ONE region's labor matching drifted at week 2 (1,449 firms' vacancyShare, one employee at a time), and the differ walked it back to the literal in three probes. **Declared per §7.313: dumps lose the field — 4wk and 13wk differ by EXACTLY one family, stripped logs identical, tests green; baselines re-based (zz4/zz13). Next fields by the same method: ratingHistory, historicalPrices, oasSpreadBpsHistory (rings), then productLines (the seam's CSR becomes the authority), then historicalFundamentals/dealerConsensus, then the scalars — at which point the store IS the company and the object is a view.**
331. **THREE MORE FIELDS COLUMNAR BY THE §7.330 METHOD — ratingHistory, historicalPrices, oasSpreadBpsHistory — the Company object no longer carries any price/rating history.** A generic `F64Ring` on V2World (52/16/8 slots; ratings as interned codes) with the exact `[...slice(-(cap-1)), x]` push; merger spins COPY ring rows (structuredClone silently carried the arrays before — the §7.312 aliasing class, pre-empted this time); seed literals stash and drain both at state assembly and at the RUNTIME birth pushes (generatePrivateCompanies runs for quarterly births too); the seed-time composite-index call's ring-absent fallback is provably equal to the old length-1 array's. Casts hid THREE sites from tsc again (carriers, generatePrivateCompanies — §7.330's lesson now twice-confirmed: after the census, grep the field name). Gates: dumps lose EXACTLY the three families at 4 and 13 weeks, stripped logs identical, tests green, baselines re-based. Weekly allocation kill so far from the four ring fields: ~10k arrays/wk. Timing pulses (single-run, ±80 ms noise on this box: 1,366 → 1,445 across the batch) say nothing yet — the battery at the stage boundary decides (§4.C). NEXT in the field queue: productLines (63 read sites / 18 files — staged per §7.311: store + dual-write + check, then readers file-by-file, then the declared delete), historicalFundamentals + dealerConsensus, then the 85 scalars, at which point the object is a view and the write-back's double-write dies.
332. **STAGE I IS CLOSED — every item done or dispositioned, and its boundary battery caught a real +62 ms/wk regression that two named fixes then erased.** Landed since the §7.327 five: 07b and 07d demand builds on dense columns (byte-identical); the ClearingResult INT FLIP in three slices — accumulateShard writes dense views only, the 07b/07d/07e apply passes read them (piById for filtered funds; has()-checks become matrix-nonzero under the engine's own >$1 fill guard), and the string maps became LAZY getters built in the exact old insertion order with an epoch guard that fails loudly on a stale read (it never fired over 13 weeks — every consumer reads its result before the next book clears); DIRECT-TO-PACK for the three fat books — an engine-owned reused demand staging in the pack's own layout (claim a row, write scalars, the pack blits), deleting ~300–500k weekly ParticipantDemand objects. Item 5 (the corp-action walk's ~17 ms) is DISPOSITIONED, not skipped: the walk is already fused and int-keyed — the remainder is chain-walk floor, and the correct fix (a maintained (type, instrument) transpose on the holdings store) binds to Stage V's store work; §1.23's one bounded attempt was spent in §7.327 (2). **The boundary battery (interleaved vs 526a2b1, §7.307 protocol) first measured HEAD +62 ms/wk NON-OVERLAPPING — a real regression inside the new machinery: (a) the dense scratch's full n×p zero-fill per book (tens of millions of writes a week) — now zeroed by replaying only the cells the last book WROTE; (b) applyDealerDeskFills materializing the whole lazy holdings map for every book — now reads the matrix when the caller hands piById. Re-battery: base 1,427 vs head 1,447, RANGES OVERLAPPING — the regression is erased and Stage I lands cost-neutral-to-noise on this box (±30 ms single-run noise; the §7.327 −89 remains the stage's measured win). The §1.23 honesty line: the int flip's and direct-to-pack's expected single-digit wins are not resolvable above this machine's noise floor — recorded as such, not claimed.** NEXT per §4.C's exact order: Stage II resumes at productLines (§7.311 staging: store + dual-write + check, readers file-by-file, declared delete), then historicalFundamentals/dealerConsensus, then the scalars.
333. **THE BENCH SHELL AND THE DELIVERY CHAIN (user-ordered): the product UI is deleted, App.tsx is a measurement instrument, and the engine now runs in real browsers on the user's phone from a URL.** The 21-file `src/components/` tree is DELETED (f277780; git history is the only copy) and `App.tsx` is a bench: Run/Stop/Restart/Copy over a monospace report (ua, cores, crossOriginIsolated, heap, weeks, steady MEDIAN + mean + min/max, per-stage means with % share, per-week list, world-digest checkpoints at weeks 1/5/10/20/50 — Σ(stockPrice+cash), full precision). **The browser build had been broken since native-kernels landed** (its top-level `import { createRequire } from 'node:module'` fails rollup): fixed by a vite alias to `src/shims/node-module-browser.ts` plus `define: {'process.env': {}}` (every engine debug flag reads off in the bundle), base `'./'`, `dist/` tsc-excluded. **Second browser crash class: a non-isolated browser HIDES the SharedArrayBuffer global** — company-store's lane allocator threw on first Run (silently: week 0, no error — the bench now prints ERROR + stack into the report); fixed with `globalThis.SharedArrayBuffer ?? ArrayBuffer` (identical single-threaded; dumps byte-identical 4/13wk). Delivery, all push-to-main automatic: `deploy-pages.yml` → https://gunsandsalvi.github.io/Test-app/ (Pages had to be enabled once by the user in Settings — a workflow token cannot create the Pages site); PWA manifest + generated icons (installable; Chrome mints a WebAPK). Stats hardening after the first phone reports: MEDIAN headline and stage means over non-outlier weeks only (>3× median excluded, count printed) — phones throttle backgrounded tabs into 3–10 s weeks that poison a mean.
334. **BROWSER MULTITHREADING EXISTS AND IS THE CAMPAIGN'S FIRST POSITIVE PARALLEL MEASUREMENT — a Web Worker twin of the clearing pool, digest-verified identical, ~20% off the 07x sum on the user's 7-core phone.** A browser forbids the Node pool twice: `Atomics.wait` is banned on the main thread, and a worker's postMessage result CANNOT be delivered mid-step (the synchronous week never yields). The twin (`clearing-worker-pool-web.ts` + `clearing-worker-web.ts`) keeps the sharding and the ordered, bit-identical accumulation and changes only transport: jobs go out by postMessage (workers' loops are free), each worker COPIES its shard into its own SAB output region behind a fixed layout, completion rings an Atomics doorbell at offset 0, and the main thread does a bounded spin (10 s timeout → pool dead → serial fallback); a 'ready' handshake keeps jobs off workers whose module hasn't evaluated; opt-in registration at runtime always lands after the Node pool's import-time self-registration (workerCount 0 in browsers). Cross-origin isolation on static hosts via the vendored `coi-serviceworker` (SW adds COOP/COEP, one auto-reload). VERIFIED: world digests bit-identical serial vs workers at wk 1/5/10 (headless, 3 workers) and wk 1/5/10/20/50 (the user's phone, 6 workers, two 85-week runs). MEASURED: headless 4-core container steady mean 1,944 → 1,693 ms; on-device 07x clearing sum ~320 → ~254 ms/wk with the workers run starting on the hotter phone. Bench defaults to `hardwareConcurrency` workers (user-ordered); the dropdown keeps max/max−1/4/2/off because the spinning main thread contends with the Nth worker — measure, don't assume. **What this changes for Stage III: the go/no-go now has a real-hardware precedent and a measurement device; the front pool (stage 08, the 16% term) is the next-biggest prize and stays Node-only until ported by the same recipe.**
335. **THE APK, AND WHAT THE ON-DEVICE CAMPAIGN MEASURED — WebView has no SharedArrayBuffer (a hard platform fact, not a config), the TWA is the correct shell, and the phone's numbers re-rank nothing but re-scale everything.** APK v1 wrapped the URL in a WebView: installed, ran serially, and could never show the worker path — Android WebView simply does not ship SAB. v2 is a Trusted Web Activity (`androidbrowserhelper` LauncherActivity): the app runs Chrome's engine fullscreen; `gunsandsalvi/gunsandsalvi.github.io` (user-created; the token cannot create repos) serves `.well-known/assetlinks.json` for the committed self-sign key (`android/keystore.p12`, deliberately committed: stable signature, installs-over; a bench app, not a secret), CI (`android-apk.yml`) publishes to the fixed `apk-latest` release, and the shell rebuilds only on `android/**` changes — the bench inside updates via Pages. MEASURED on the 7-core phone (Chrome 153): app ≥ browser (median 1,015 vs 1,083 ms/wk, app run started cooler — read "at least as fast"); serial first-visit run 953 ms/wk mean vs the dev box's 1,305 battery — **the phone is the faster machine and the deployment target; dev-box absolute numbers under-state it by ~25%**. The stage PROFILE matches Node (05 ~26%, 08 ~16-17%, then the 07x books) — §4.C's targets transfer unchanged. LESSONS with teeth: (1) cross-BUILD digests diverge in the last digits past ~wk 5 (`Math.exp/pow` are implementation-defined per V8 build) — digests compare runs WITHIN one device/build only, never across; (2) the growth drift is REAL state growth, not thermals — two independent 85-week runs track the same +45% curve weeks 5→80 (§6.2 row); (3) `performance.memory` is quantized garbage without COI and real with it.
336. **DIRECTIVE CHANGE AND HANDOVER (user, 2026-09-01 evening): §4.C/SCALE is PARKED as later-TBD mid-Stage-II; AU — the UI rebuild — is THE NEXT PROJECT.** The park is an ORDER, not a judgment: Stage I closed (§7.332), Stage II's store runs on trust with four fields columnar (§7.329–331), and the resume point is written in the §5 SCALE banner (productLines by the §7.311 staging, then historicalFundamentals/dealerConsensus, then the 85 scalars, then III ∥ IV → V → VI → VII). Two facts recorded today feed the eventual resume: the browser clearing pool's on-device win (§7.334 — the first positive parallel measurement, directly relevant to III's go/no-go) and the state-growth drift (§6.2 — long-run cost is not flat, so §4.C's per-week arithmetic has a time dimension nobody priced). AU starts from §5 AU as rewritten today: deletion done, delivery chain live, design brief FIRST — the user approves the brief before anything is built. Handover mechanics for a fresh session are in §4's HANDOVER STATE block (branch, both-push rule, gates, regenerate-baselines-at-session-start — the scratchpad is ephemeral and the engine deterministic). The bench (App.tsx + Pages + PWA + APK) is the user's instrument: AU must keep it reachable, and any engine code that reaches the browser bundle keeps the §7.333 guard discipline (bare `process` behind `typeof`, SAB behind availability checks, no top-level Node imports on the bundle path).
337. **DRV — THE ONE DERIVATIVE LAYER (user-ordered 2026-09-01, ahead of AU): a derivative is a CONTRACT, a class is its PROFILE, hedging is one arithmetic, capacity is one budget — and the rebuild found two defects the four-times-written lifecycle had hidden.** What stood before: four contract structs, three party unions re-encoding the ledger's `PartyRef`, four books in four places (`reg.swapBook`, `reg.cdsBook`, `state.commodityFuturesBook`, `entity/company.fxForwards`), three desk-capacity formulas for one balance sheet, hedging vocabulary written four ways, no counterparty close-out on any book. Now (`domain/derivatives/`, `stages/derivative-lifecycle.ts`): (1) ONE contract shape (two named parties in class-named roles, notional, strike in the class's own stat, reference + tenor key, `settledMarkUSD`) on ONE book (`GameState.derivativesBook`); (2) a class is a `DerivativeClassProfile` — exactly one leg family (PERIODIC: swap net, CDS premium; MARK: futures VM, FX forward VM — §7.241's delta rule owned by the lifecycle, never a profile), an event termination (credit event, a reference that stopped existing), a close-out, and two facts (Basel-CEM PFE add-on, initial-margin rate); the registry is the dispatch table and is compile-loud until a new class is named — **adding a derivative is one profile module + one registry line**; (3) ONE lifecycle, invoked per class at the pipeline point where its prints are fresh: event → maturity (final leg) → COUNTERPARTY DEATH (a DEFAULTED party's contracts close out at replacement value through its account — the estate's account IS the debtor's, §7.286; a GONE party ends the contract flat) → live legs; (4) ONE hedging arithmetic (`hedging.ts`: the walk-away is the risk removed at what carrying it costs — the FX tolerance and the futures concession were the same rule in two vocabularies) with the §7.241 standing-cover index written ONCE over the one book; (5) ONE desk-capacity budget (`DESK_DERIVATIVE_PFE_SHARE_OF_HEADROOM` × leverage headroom, charged by every live contract a bank stands on at its class's add-on — closing dealer-derivatives.ts's recorded open question; the CDS desk's `equity/0.08`, the storage desk's copy and the FX-only `fxDeskCapacityUSD` all dead); (6) the merger re-key is one map over one book (the old per-book re-keys MISSED the futures book and re-keyed only the FX forward's bank side). **THE TWO DEFECTS FOUND BY REBUILDING (rule 14's class, both invisible to every check):** (a) the futures book's weekly variation margin was structurally ZERO — its settle pass marked non-expiring positions at their own last mark and the end-of-stage re-mark moved the mark with no cash leg, so only the delivery week's single move ever paid: a long that gained 50/unit over a contract's life and gave it back in the last week collected nothing, on every contract, for the book's whole life; the cumulative-mark-settled-as-delta form now telescopes to (delivery spot − strike) × units in real cash (pinned by test). (b) the corporate FX hedger's margin budget read `c.cashUSD` on an `any`-typed Company (the field is `cash`) — always undefined, so corporates could post margin only from pending settlement; typing the stage found it. Also folded: the player-trade layer's flat FX add-on now reads the registry by asset type. **THE RECKONING (user directive: test only at the end):** tsc clean; lint 372 → 356 (ratchet lowered); hygiene clean; tests 98 → 108 (the profiles' legs, the telescoping mark, close-out = carry, one budget across classes, the standing-cover index, registry completeness); `WEEKS=4` and `WEEKS=13` SHOCKS=0 runs HARNESS PASSED with ZERO violations every week — exact parity with the pre-DRV tree (0/0 at both horizons); world in family at w13 (u 18.4/12.5/11.1/10.5 vs 18.3/12.6/11.0/10.1; GDP 0.98T both; 10Y 4.70 both; CPI 81.6 vs 82.6); damper-by-book shows every class clearing (cds 29, commodity 11, fx 5 vs 23/9/4). DECLARED RELABEL (§7.313): every derivative leg's float path changed and the dumps lose four book families; `zz`-baselines re-based on this tree (scratchpad `gates/drv-4.json`, `drv-13.json`). Harness census block `--- DRV: the one derivative book ---` added; its first read (6wk probe, week 7): 4,111 live contracts — IRS 502 / 381.1B, futures 3,113 / 21.3B with **2.7B of mark SETTLED to the longs** (the cash that never moved before), FX forwards 492 / 385.4B, CDS 4 / 0.2B — PFE charged to bank desks 10.1B on the one budget. Remainders in §5-DRV, each a registry flip or a named gate: IM on the non-FX classes (a policy call, one number per profile), the CDS add-on by rating, a dealer between the IRS/CDS/futures sides, an options CLASS (two profile methods; its market is the work), swap lines (§7.282's gate). THE LESSON: **when the same lifecycle is written N times, the N-th copy is where the leg goes missing — the futures VM bug could not exist in a lifecycle written once, and the merger re-key gap could not exist on one book.**
338. **LVL — THE PRICE LEVEL, AND THE DAMPER THAT ADAPTS (the campaign's first cluster, user-ordered 2026-09-01: "start from inflation issues and instrument bounds").** Diagnosis by instrument, not by reading: SPIRAL_PRICES showed every household good opening ×1.3–1.4 a week with supply/demand 0.5–0.8; SEED_RECON showed demand plans consistent with the level; PX_FOCUS + a new seller-side CAT_TRACE line showed the software collapse. Three defects, three owners: **(a) the want-vs-budget wedge was the seed price rule** — `deriveSubUnitUnitPrice` divided all final dollars by a volume that weighted the population's want by the buyer mix, so a household's money bought a fraction of its registry want at the seed price and every market opened short in UNITS while supply was sized in DOLLARS; the price of a household good is now household dollars over the household's physical want (week-1 inflation print 49 → 15). **(b) coverage at sub-unit grain** — the greedy line deal left household chemicals at 0.67 and consumer software at 0.04 of demand; an iterative proportional fit of each firm's line SHARES against demand net of what the pools really carry (§7.227's discipline) makes coverage exact where the roster can reach it, conserving every firm's revenue and headcount. **(c) the full-cost shutdown was a bang-bang rule** — one week of dearer input lots against last week's price idled every consumer-software producer in every region at once (unit cost 204 → 376 vs 325 expected; supply 1.19M → 0.08M; price ×3 on the sliver); the short-run test and the offer floor now run on AVOIDABLE cost (inputs), with the ask sliding from full cost + margin (nothing in stock) down to avoidable cost (warehouse full) — IND6's posture with the floor where the short-run economics puts it; wages adjust through the labour rule, persistent losses through DYN's mothball. **(d) the household demand curve is a staircase over the cohorts** — the one regional ladder truncated to a single reach-capped step (§7.209's fix flattened it), so a short staple market jumped straight to the reach multiple; each cohort now posts its own step at its own budget and reach, a measured slope with no stated elasticity (§7.157). **(e) the damper adapts** — the cap widens by (1 + streak) for a name bound in one direction week after week (`damperBindStreak`, a packed lane; streak memory on `GameState.damperBindStreakById`, rolled by core; the native clearing kernel declines a widened shard and runs the JS canonical), and every bind now carries its direction in the diagnostic (`book:id±`, up/down counts per book). **Measured, 13-week probes (SHOCKS=0, harness green throughout):** CPI at 13 weeks 180 → 146 → 143; goods fill 0.687 → 0.756; persistent binds 1,364 → 973 — corporate bond 209 → 47, CP 130 → 51, CDS 34 → 2, stock loan 172 → 121 (all the credit tails were pinned UP: spreads the market wanted wider than the cap printed); equity 739 (395 up / 344 down — the small-cap float half, SCALE's, unchanged); consumer software s/d 0.04 → 0.63. Cost recorded, not chased (§1.12/§1.20): unemployment at week 13 runs 2–4 pp above the pre-LVL tree (USA 20.7 vs 18.4, EUR 16.4 vs 12.6) — lower prices reach the labour rule as lower real revenue; the 60-week reference at the campaign's end judges the endpoint. Also closed: one owner each for the labour force (`laborForceCount`), book depreciation (the programme's rule) and the seller PD (the structural model) — three of the four "one quantity, many authors" survivors; the consumer loss curves remain. Declared relabel from week 1.
339. **BANK RESOLUTION — REBUILT, IDENTITY-EXACT, AND THE BID IS LEAST-COST (2026-09-01).** §7.302's patch did not survive its scratchpad; the mechanism was rebuilt from the record, and its two recorded defects were found to be one: the assuming bank's sheet was written before settlement rebuilt it (settlement replaces a bank's sheet OBJECT; a handle taken before the pass is last week's), so the residual and the −40.6B wholesale were the same stale write. **Shape:** its own stage, after `settlement-close` (empty journal, final sheets), before the central bank counts reserves. Trigger: `isBankUnderPca` — equity < 2% of RWA (`PCA_CAPITAL_RATIO`), on the week's final sheet; stage 08's default rule never fires on a bank (its cash is reserves, its coverage a margin), which is why a bank with no capital used to trade on dead. Assuming bank: the best-capitalised live peer above the 8% floor, by equity (`chooseAssumingBank`); none → the bank stays open and says so. **The plan (`planBankResolution`, pure):** the shell's own ladder stays behind as receivership claims (bailed in — it is inside the wholesale line, so it comes out of what transfers); the assuming bank takes every asset and every deposit and secured line at book and is paid to carry the book at the working ratio (`acquirerCapitalUSD` = 11% × the failed bank's RWA — the ratio banks run at, no new constant); its bid for the net is what exceeds that, paid to the receivership; a shortfall is funded in the loss order wholesale haircut → treasury guarantee (`GOVERNMENT → BANK`, a real fiscal cost that lands in the TGA). Depositors lose nothing; the acquirer's equity ends up by exactly the capital the book needs, in every case. **The legs:** every non-cash line moves by `absorbBankSheet` (in the LEDGER — it writes every balance line; `10-mergers` now uses the same transfer via `mergeBankSheets`, which also fixes the merger's blended-scalar mortgage merge (vintages carry now), its dropped unmodeled-deposit/desk/FX/PB/coupon lines, and its dead customer links); cash by a `BANK → BANK` payment settled in an extra pass while both sheets exist (an overdrawn failed bank is made whole by the acquirer first); then the shell drops its sheet, banks at its acquirer, defaults with `bankResolvedWeek`, and the receivership payment lands on its account for next week's estate. **Every link re-keys** (`rekeyBankLinks`, shared with mergers): customers' and institutions' house bank, facility rows' `bankRef`, the repo and prime-brokerage books, the offering pipeline, the derivatives book, the treasury's accrued-coupon ledger (missed at first: the calendar re-derived the acquirer's receivable from its own key and the failed bank's 34M vanished — and its coupons kept being paid to a bank with no sheet). **Two things the rebuild taught:** desks hold ONE position per name — carrying a failed bank's rows beside the acquirer's own for the same instrument left phantoms the clearing stages could not see, and the week after broke the identity by 29.8B across five books (rows now sum per name, which is gross-neutral for same-sign rows and what a single desk position means); and the least-cost bid is not a nicety — paying the receivership the full net handed the failed bank's shareholders 5.1B and dropped the acquirer to 5.7% in five weeks (now: 0.12B to the receivership — its ladder — and the acquirer holds 10.3%). **Measured:** `BANK_RESOLUTION_FORCE=SAXW@3` (instrument, inert unless set): resolution week and the seven after harness-green, zero unresolved, the shell at cash 0 / D / no staff, the acquirer's household deposits 51 → 92B. Settlement gained an `UNRESOLVED_TRACE` line for a leg addressed to a bank with no sheet, naming the reason. Unit tests pin the loss order, the ladder cap, the identity of the transfer and the acquirer's capital gain (`test/bank-resolution.test.ts`).
340. **THE FUNDING CLOSE — a bank short of its buffer at the close raises wholesale money, and no bank ends a week overdrawn at the central bank (2026-09-01).** Found by §7.339's dump: MIUJ, ADFI, VQFA closed week 13 with reserves of −1.06B, −0.44B, −0.47B (SAXW −0.74B at week 3) — an unsecured overdraft at the central bank that nothing lends, cost nothing (reserves earn `max(0, cash)`), and the harness did not watch. The roll only ever fell (§7.254's `unrenewedWholesaleUSD`); a bank the repo session could not fund (collateral gone) had no way to raise money. **Built:** `raiseWholesaleUSD` (bank-lending.ts, the liability's owner) — shortfall to the operating buffer (2% of deposits, the repo session's own target) written onto the wholesale line; a new stage `bank-funding-close` after `settlement-close` posts the cash leg (UNMODELED → BANK_SECURITIES, 'wholesale funding raised', a declared frontier beside 'repaid') and settles it in its own pass. **At the close and not in 02b**, because the shortfall is made AFTER 02b — the desks buy inventory sized by capital, and a week's customer settlement takes tens of billions out of one bank (MIUJ −25B by week 3 in the first probe; a raise struck in the morning funded 4.8B of it). The boundary lender pays out of its deposits at the region's banks (settlement spreads the debit by share), so one round leaves every bank a little shorter; the close iterates to eight rounds and converges past the dollar. Harness: `Bank X overdrawn at the central bank` is a violation now. **Found on the way:** a private-equity fund closed a week at −2.9M on 960M — the CP book's per-issuer limit (5% of the sleeve) is a concentration rule, not a budget, and fifty issuers' bids offered the same dollar 2.5× (the engine has no cross-instrument budget); the sleeve is now divided across the bids (`min(5%, 1/N)` each), so the sum can never exceed it. **Measured, 13 weeks SHOCKS=0, harness green:** no overdrawn bank in any week; wholesale raised 134B USA / 20B EUR / 6B JPN / 0.6B UK, 113B of it MIUJ (§6.1 watch); CPI 143 → 148, goods fill 0.756 → 0.742, u w13 20.4/16.6/11.7/11.0 (was 20.7/16.4/11.9/11.0), 10Y 5.01 (was 5.14), persistent binds 986 → 969 — the price of funding what used to be free, recorded not chased (§1.12).
341. **THE SMALL ITEMS, THE CRE MARKET REOPENED, AND A FUND'S SPENDABLE CASH (2026-09-02).** Five closes and one rule, each measured. **(a)** The consumer loss curve is one curve (the no-tier-books branch was a second formula), and the instalment/revolving loss ratio has one owner (`CONSUMER_TERM_LOSS_SHARE_OF_CARD`; three `× 0.5` sites). **(b)** The CDS PFE add-on is by rating (5% IG / 10% below, CEM) through `pfeAddOnRateFor` on the profile and `isInvestmentGrade` on the market view; the flat rate stays the capacity denominator. **(c)** The FX spread's last payers: households and treasuries buying abroad pay the same 2bps pip to the buyer region's desks (HOUSEHOLD/GOVERNMENT party → BANK). **(d)** The manager/vehicle link resolves through `managedEntityIdsOf` everywhere (the seed's last `e.id === comp.id` is gone); the vehicle's own id is a seed re-base, bundled under SEED_BURN_IN (§6.1). **(e)** The CRE market is LIVE: the reopening condition (the level row, §7.338) was met, and 30 weeks live vs gated show no spiral (CPI 159.2 vs 158.9, fill 0.763 vs 0.754, u 29.6/28.8/24.6/20.8 vs 28.9/31.2/23.1/25.3); `CRE_MARKET_LIVE=0` gates it off. **(f) THE SPENDABLE RULE.** The small-cap ETF ran ~10M overdrawn for fifteen weeks from week 15. Traced (`BANK_IDENTITY_TRACE_INSTITUTION`): an in-kind redemption's cash slice paid out ALL its cash, then a stock loan closed and 1.42M of collateral had to go back; the week after, 27.7M more. The collateral a lender HOLDS sits in `cashUSD` beside its own money, and every book sized a bid on `cash + pending` — so the fund bought equity with borrowers' collateral, redeemed it away, and returned it from nothing. One rule now: `institutionSpendableUSD` = cash + pending − stock-loan collateral held (memoised per loan-book array), at every budget site (07b/c/d/e/f, repo, securities lending, ETF creations, redemptions and fees); the class-budget rule takes the netted pending. Also: the ETF's worst-case price reads the damper's widened cap (`damperStreakOf`), the harness's institutional-book check is 10% not 5% (a marked book can re-mark 6.7% in a week under the adaptive cap; a missing leg is tens of per cent), and the CP sleeve fix (§7.340) had already bounded the PE fund. **The 30-week picture, recorded not chased (§1.12):** pre-session tree (dbbd054) — 1 violation at w30 (USA u 30.3% > band), pi ~100; post-LVL (02ea4ec) — 39 in 10 families (UK/USA/JPN bank NIM and capital bands, USA/EUR u > 30%, the ETF); this tree — 22 in 6 families: UK bank NIM ×9 and capital ×7 (the UK system runs at negative NIM from week 20 — WGJI and THSY resolved into VOUL, whose own wholesale is priced at its blown-out spread: §6.1's UK watch, now with resolution behind it), USA u > 30% ×3 (w28–30; 31.0/26.2/26.4/20.8 at w30), one EUR book re-mark > 10%, one USA and one JPN NIM week. No overdrawn fund, no overdrawn bank, every identity closed. Fill 0.777 and CPI 158 at 30 weeks (LVL's trade: the pre-session tree had CPI ~200 and the same u). The 60-week shocks-on reference at the campaign's end is the judge.
342. **AU SLICE 1 IS BUILT; THE DAMPER'S WIDENING IS BOUNDED; THE LAST BANK IS RECAPITALISED; AND THE 60-WEEK REFERENCE IS RECORDED (2026-09-02).** **(a) Aurora.** `src/ui/`: `world.ts` (the resolver — ticker, name, id, region; every identifier on screen resolves or is plain text — the register read as holders/holdings, the engine's rings, and the TAPE: the UI's own weekly recorder for regional prints and institution book sizes, so no engine state grows for a view), `calendar.ts` (week 0 = 1 Jan 2027; dates, months, quarters — never a week), `ui.tsx` (the approved design's primitives), `fn.ts` + `functions/` (one module per function, `appliesTo` per type: overview, all, chart, statements, holders/holdings, peers), `Aurora.tsx` (the phone shell: one panel, a back stack per panel, swipe between panels, long-press a name to open it beside, the command bar with autocomplete and `<object> <function> <arg>` parsing — `usa chart 10y`, `emwf all bankBalanceSheet` — the clock: step / run 3 months / run / stop, recents in localStorage). `main.tsx` routes `#bench` to the bench. The five UI flags left `GameState` (rule 3's last open row). Smoke-tested in Chromium at 390×844: home, region/company/bank overviews, statements (quarter beside quarter, bank sheet with its identity residual printed), holders, peers, all (nested links, path breadcrumbs), chart with the scrubber after two steps. One CSS lesson: a scrolling column flexbox shrinks an `overflow: hidden` child to nothing when the column is full — `.aurora-body > * { flex-shrink: 0 }`. **(b) The widening is bounded** (`DAMPER_STREAK_WIDENING_MAX = 3`): §7.338's (1+k)× cap, unbounded, let a book that binds every week move 20× the flat cap by week 20; the first 60-week reference had JPN's institutional book re-marking ×4 a week from week 55 (21T → 8,000T) and JPN unemployment at 80%. Bounded: JPN u 29.6% at w60, the region's book a book again. **(c) The last bank standing is recapitalised by its treasury** (`resolution: public recapitalisation`, GOVERNMENT → BANK to the working ratio) when no peer can assume it — JPN's CLFP twice (w42, w43; 32B in all); shareholders are not diluted (no share mechanics on a bank's equity — recorded). **(d) The reference, 60 weeks shocks-on, this tree:** u 9.5/3.7/2.9/3.0 (w1) → 27.0/24.2/17.4/16.1 (w20) → 32.0/29.1/22.4/24.1 (w30) → 39.9/32.8/30.0/29.6 (w60); nine resolutions (UK VOUL→GCKB w20, THSY→GCKB w32; USA PGNX→MIUJ w16, MIUJ→EMWF w21, ADFI→EMWF w41; EUR SAXW→IVQH w19; JPN TJBT/XIVF/VQFA→CLFP w39) and the two recapitalisations; the harness red on the bank NIM/capital bands and the u band from week ~20. The world is in a depression with a banking crisis on every tree since LVL (the pre-session tree reached USA u 30.3% at w30 with CPI ~200). §1.12 held; the row above names the next campaign's first item and its owner.
343. **THE BUYERS' RESERVATION RATCHET (2026-09-02).** The user's quick test still showed the level exploding after LVL. Traced by SPIRAL_PRICES + SEED_RECON on a 13-week probe: every buyer class other than households anchored its reservation to LAST WEEK'S PRINT — a corporate at reference × (0.95..1.05) × a cash-rich premium (+15%), a treasury at reference × 1.5 (`GOVERNMENT_BID_PRICE_TOLERANCE`) — with the quantity inelastic, so a market short by 25% cleared at the top block each week and the block moved up with it: a ratchet, one notch a week, on the biggest budgets in the market. **The fix is one curve for everyone:** `householdDemandLadder` is `budgetDemandLadder` (`DEMAND_LADDER_RUNGS = 3`), and a corporate, a pool recipe, a capex plan and a treasury each post the same ladder from their own dollar budget under a MEASURED cap (reach × the going price; corporate reach = 1 + EBITDA margin / input intensity, capex and government reach 1) — `cashRichPricePremium` and the tolerance deleted. The ladder's rungs are the budget's own hyperbola (`budget / (step·i)`), not the reach-truncated flat that §7.209's fix had left (which posted the whole-want reservation on every rung — one inelastic block). **Measured, 13 weeks SHOCKS=0:** CPI 142.9 (base 151), goods fill 0.697, one violation (USA bank NIM 0.0093, the UK/EUR watch's sibling); a first flat six-rung ladder made it WORSE (166, 5 s/wk) — the hyperbola under the cap fixed both. Essentials USA converge (25.9 → 39, then flat) where every prior tree compounded. **What remains is a level, not a spiral, and it is the roster's:** essentials USA firm capacity 19.8M + pools ≈ 20.9M units against 26.9M gross demand — `normalizeProducingSectorRevenue` fits at sector grain and `dealProductLinesAndHeadcount`'s IPF fits SHARES within a firm (ratio clamped [0.25, 4]), so nothing sets a sub-unit's revenue LEVEL to the demand it faces, and no live rule adds capacity to a 30% margin. **The direction decided with the user (2026-09-02, discussion, no code):** the one-by-one seed list stops; a seed cannot hold a world whose engine has no negative feedback (every tree since LVL reaches the same depression by week 30 — §7.342). Order: this ladder (demand answers price, in); two preference primitives per deciding entity, patience and risk aversion, drawn at seed and replaced on measured failure (§5-MGMT), with every constant threshold re-expressed through measured state and those two numbers — heterogeneity of thresholds, not smarter agents (uniform clever agents synchronise harder), no style tables, no injected noise; supply entry and exit and a wage response as live rules; then the burn-in gate as the seed's self-correction (structure seeded, the engine relaxes a year shocks-off with defaults and resolution frozen, the harness bands the convergence test; what still drifts is a §6 engine defect, never a seed patch). §1.12 is unaffected: the burn-in is the test, not a mid-project read.
344. **BRAINS — THE PREFERENCE PAIR ON EVERY DECIDING ENTITY, THE THRESHOLDS RE-EXPRESSED THROUGH IT, AND MANAGEMENT TURNOVER (2026-09-02, user-ordered after the §7.343 discussion).** The user's diagnosis was right and is now the charter (§5-BRAINS): identical rule-followers with identical constants cross every threshold in the same week, so aggregates that should be averages were cliffs. The build, under rule 19: `domain/preferences.ts` — patience as a horizon in weeks (log-uniform on [4, 52], median √(4·52) ≈ 14.4, the quarter) and risk aversion relative to 1 (log-uniform on [0.5, 2]) — drawn ONCE per entity from its own scoped stream (`mgmt:<id>` salted by the week of appointment, so the global stream never moves and a replay is identical), for every company (firms, banks, the shells that manage funds), every institutional entity and every household cohort (re-struck from its identity each rebuild). **The median brain is yesterday's rule**: every threshold that was a constant is now that constant at the median, scaled by the entity's own two numbers — 19 sites, listed in §5-BRAINS (the labour affordability test on an adaptive EBITDA expectation at the management's horizon; the required return's premium, cut/hire speeds, the operating buffer, the rate drag, the cash-health threshold, the CFO's action threshold and paces, the hedge's sigma count, the buyer's cash-rationing threshold, a bank's hurdle and cash buffer, a holder's hurdle, overweight and full-size range, a cohort's buffer weeks and spend-down years — through risk aversion; the reinvestment envelope, the capex stock-adjustment weight, the payout ceiling and the buyer's reference price — the mean of the last `patience` prints — through patience). One owner per new fact: the labour stage writes `expectedEbitdaUSD`, stage 08 reads it through a lane; `management-review` (quarterly) draws every brain at entry (the catch-up convention core.ts already uses for ladders and books) and replaces a firm's after four failed reviews (expected margin below its own baseline AND its region-sector peers' median — the definitional midpoint, no coefficient), with a news item naming the outgoing pair and the incoming one. No style table, no rule branch by kind, no injected noise. **Measured, 13 weeks SHOCKS=0 against §7.343's tree:** 0 violations (was 1), CPI 143.6 (142.9), goods fill 0.716 (0.697), u 20.3/18.8/12.7/11.5 at w13 (USA 20.7, EUR 16.4 on the LVL tree) — the centre unmoved, as designed; the first review (w13) judged 2,345 firms, 977 failing, none yet replaced (a year is four reviews; on the burn-in's w52 review 142 were replaced). 7 tests pin the ranges, the median, the determinism and the expectation arithmetic. DIST-P: +2 stated ranges, the only two shapes this adds. Declared relabel from week 1 (every management reads its own numbers). Remainders in §5-BRAINS.
345. **THE BURN-IN IS THE INSTRUMENT: SIX RULES WRONG ON THEIR OWN TERMS, FOUND BY RUNNING THE ENGINE TO ITS OWN FIXED POINT (2026-09-02, user-ordered with §7.344).** `burn-in.ts` rebuilt to the user's ask: not §7.294's fixed twelve weeks but a run to a CONVERGENCE TEST (`SEED_BURN_IN=auto`: every watched quantity — u and CPI by region, goods fill, active firms — inside a week's noise for 8 consecutive weeks; 156 weeks at most), calendar CONTINUOUS (`burnInWeeks` on the state, the UI subtracts; §7.294's `currentWeek: 0` reset was what broke the meeting cycle), a trace naming what still moves, and two cause instruments (DEFAULT_TRACE, LABOR_CAUSES). Then it was run, and it did what it is for. **The first run (§7.344's tree): total collapse** — u 30/45/23/22 at w20, 75/86/70/80 at w52, CPI 1.2e5/1.3e7/3.9e4/5.9e5, fill 0.000, 1,094 of 2,490 firms left, 800 dead by w28, nine bank resolutions; the harness crashed on the numbers. Read in order with the instruments (each a rule contradicting itself, each fixed at its owner, none a rollback — §1.20): **(1) the flat ladder, again** — §7.343 kept §7.209's cap at the whole-want reservation on EVERY rung, so a buyer with reach ≈ 1 (EUR's households) posted three rungs at the going price and a short market could ration but never reprice: CAT_TRACE showed EUR apparel 40% → 80% short at a FLAT print (67 → 64) while the equally short USA market cleared at 150–172; EUR's consumer firms starved (sector margin 19.5% → 1.5% in twelve weeks, 70 of 167 loss-making, 140 dead by w17 — DEFAULT_TRACE). The rungs are the reachable budget's own hyperbola now (`reachable / (step·i)`; the last rung IS the reservation); the rung count samples the curve (a resolution choice) and the clearing point is the curve's. Alone this ended the death wave: firms 1,781 → 2,437 at w24, fill 0.58 → 0.84, EUR deaths 230 → 24. **(2) produce to sales, not to the warehouse** — the throttle bit only past the warehouse, so a firm in an oversupplied market ran its plant flat out into seven weeks of unsold stock (UUMT: 1.8M units of apparel against 268k/wk) and died of cash; a plant now makes what its management EXPECTS to sell (adaptive, at its horizon; per-line sales recorded where they settle) plus the good's own production lead of stock (a registry primitive, no target-inventory constant), and the plant it does not run is slack the capacity-retirement rule integrates. **(3) the affordability cut fired productive workers to close an earnings shortfall** and outranked the level target — a cut that lowers earnings cannot close an earnings gap; it is bounded to the heads output does not need (the cash rule still sheds unconditionally). **(4) cost-of-living recovery without bargaining power** — the going rate recovered 60% of inflation with a third of the workforce out of work; it is weighted by the matching function's own job-finding rate now (§7.210's one representation; 1 at neutral tightness, 0 with none). **(5) the deflator carried a different lag from what it deflated** (rule 9) — a revenue EMA at 0.08 divided by the instant print read a one-month repricing as a quarter of real collapse; `smoothedPriceAt` reads the print with the revenue's own weight at both ends of the window. **(6) hiring gated on the plant's return** — at a 10% ten-year yield the cause trace read ZERO vacancies in every region at 25% unemployment; a hire now pays for itself on value added per head against the wage, toward the level target, cash permitting. Also: the mothball/scrap clocks and the sales expectation run on the management's horizon (§5-BRAINS); entry goes to every pool where entering pays, not the argmax; the firm birth's pool debit stays with settlement (a double-count caught by hygiene). **Measured, the final tree:** 13 weeks plain — u 22.2/16.4/15.3/14.8, one violation, fill 0.816, CPI ×2.78/1.94/2.45/2.54 (the seed's coverage gap — §7.343's 72% — priced ONCE at budget/supply, as the ladder says it must: the USA month-one print is the level, not a spiral), firms below their cost of capital 4.6/7.1/5.4/8.5% of the roster where the seed put half, EBITDA/revenue 28–36%. The burn-in to 156 weeks does NOT settle: u 27.4/24.3/23.5/23.8 (w24) → 35.0/35.0/31.1/46.0 (w60) → 39.8/46.2/41.7/52.9 (w108); → 45.3/55.2/47.7/63.9 (w156, NOT SETTLED: USA/EUR/JPN CPI and EUR/JPN u still moving); CPI 685/554/835/2,971 at w108 and 1,097/963/1,283/6,278 at w156; fill 0.85 → 0.49 → 0.40; firms 2,427 → 1,936 → 1,918; five bank resolutions against 41 on the previous tree; managements replaced 56–88 per review from w104. **What still drifts, named by the instruments (the next campaign's first item, owner LAB/LVL):** from w13 the only layoff cause is the GROWTH signal and vacancies are zero in every region — the level target deflates a budget-anchored (nominal-fixed) revenue by a price that rose ×2.5 and reads every firm as overstaffed by half, while the price keeps rising ~40%/yr with fill FALLING: the household reach multiple counts the same reallocatable money once per category (phantom demand above what cash can pay), and a firm's input budget is a share of its own nominal revenue (no nominal anchor on the corporate side). §1.12 held: one A/B (the mothball clock, identical trajectories) and the cause traces; everything else was built, then measured. SEED_BURN_IN stays OFF on this measurement, as §7.294 left it, and is now the gate's own test. Declared relabel from week 1.
346. **NEWS SLICE 1 AND AURORA SLICE 2 (2026-09-02, user: "do the news update and the Aurora update, I want to play test").** **News is derived, never invented** (`stages/news-derivation.ts`, before the feed is assembled): a default story carries the firm's books at the moment (cash, coverage, revenue, debt, people, house bank) and its WHY off the payment journal — the week's largest outflows by reason and payee ("this week it paid 12M of interest to VOUL, 30M of inputs to …"); rating crossings of the IG line and two-notch moves are stories, the rest a per-region digest (the notch drift ran 75 a week and would have drowned everything — the feed keeps a quarter, 600 items); mergers, entries carved from pools, builds abroad, the first week of a mothball, a bank drawing at the window (not repeated while it continues), estates closed with the recovery; each region's labour market when it moves half a point (naming the largest cuts or hirers and the week's deaths) and its price level when it moves a percent (naming the categories and how much of their demand was served). Every item carries `refs` (every one a link), a `kind`, a size (`materialityUSD`) and where the ledger says so a `cause`; the older generator's weather item lost its coin-flip (a real anomaly is reported, rule 4). Two lessons: the company objects are mutated in place, so a week-on-week delta must read the engine's own previous-value fields (`previousEmployeeCount`, the mothball streak, the print's history ring), never a "previous state" that is the same object. **Aurora:** the function strip sits under the object's name (the user's "how do I use a function once I've selected an object"), `news` is a function on every type and the home page shows what happened this week, `all` reads field names aloud with the stored key beneath, the overview names things plainly (market cap, revenue trailing year, held by funds) and states the management's horizon and risk weight in words, and no screen says "weeks" (formatSpan). Verified on 390×844 screenshots via `vite preview` + playwright-core.
347. **THE ABSTRACTION CHECK (2026-09-02, user-ordered): rule 17 walked over the stages, the behaviour switches moved into registries, the lookups left alone.** Inventory: 20 literal per-kind comparisons in `stages/` and `engine2/` (`entityType === '…'`, `hedgeFundStrategy === '…'`, `sector === '…'`, `industry === '…'`). Thirteen are LOOKUPS — "the region's money fund", "the ETFs", "the PE fund", "the insurers" — a stage asking for the entities that play a role; the type is the registry and they stay. Seven were BEHAVIOUR switched on a kind, and each is now a registry fact: **(a)** the hedge-fund STRATEGY registry (`HEDGE_FUND_STRATEGY_PROFILES`, domain/institution-profiles.ts) — a strategy's targets, whether it prices credit off discounted recovery and runs conviction size (DISTRESSED: 4×, was a stage constant), shorts equity (LONG_SHORT_EQUITY), speculates in commodity futures and runs directional FX (GLOBAL_MACRO), hedges its foreign equity (every book but macro) — read by 07b/07d, securities-lending, 07i, fx-clearing and the FX-forward class; **(b)** every kind's policy allocation on `INSTITUTION_PROFILES.targets` (the seed's two tables leave initialization.ts; `allocationTargetFor(role, strategy)` is the one reader); **(c)** the first per-kind BEHAVIOUR field the profile file promised — `liabilityHurdle(entity, stated)`: an insurer's cost of float, a pension's benefit need, `entityRequiredReturn` reads it and switches on nothing; **(d)** stage 08's two lane literals — a bank is what the statement-profile resolver says (`profileKeyOf`), and the R&D share of growth investment is the INDUSTRY's financing profile (`rndShareOfGrowthCapex: 0.4` on the two tech industries, was a hard-coded `hasTechLine` 40/60 split). **Gate:** a 4-week `STATE_DUMP` byte-identical before and after (§1.22's strongest); lint at the ratchet. **Left, named:** the seed generator's sector→category ladder (`companyGenerator.ts` ~15 literals, seed code — a `SECTOR_PROFILE` table exists beside it and the ladder belongs in it); `household-portfolio.ts`'s ETF lookup (a lookup); Aurora's `overview` branching on `ref.type` inside the module (the charter's `src/ui/objects/*` object registry is the home; one module per object type when the fourth type arrives); the older news generator's category strings. **DIST-P: −1** (the distressed conviction constant is a registry row).
348. **AURORA SLICE 3 — THE DELIVERABLE REVIEWED WHOLE, THE REGISTRY MADE WIDE (2026-09-02, user: "I told you to complete the Aurora project. It's still a mess… clean each menu and sub menu, each view need to adapt to what's showing, there need to be more functions and more objects shown and if an object is loaded I should just be able to type the function to make it work, not always object + function").** What stood: three object types resolved inside `world.ts`, and `overview`/`chart`/`peers`/`statements` each a switch on `ref.type` — §7.347 had named it as the one UI abstraction gap. **Now (rule 17 for the UI, as the charter said):** `src/ui/objects/registry.ts` is the OBJECT contract — `find`, `list`, `label`, `keywords`, `parse` (the kind's own phrase), `overview`, `series`, `peers {groups, columns | columns(anchor), defaultSort}`, `headline`, `mentions` — and `src/ui/objects/index.ts` is the registry: EIGHTEEN kinds, one module each — company (a bank reads bank columns, a firm firm columns: the columns are a function of the anchor), institution, region, market (`R:subUnit` — price, fill, who buys, the sellers), pool (`R:industry`), cohort (`R:occ:tier`), occupation (`R:occ`), commodity, fx pair, curve, central bank, tranche (`owner|id`, reached from a ladder), contract (the one derivative book), offering, estate, book (a damper book by region, from the bound-ids keys), lane (`USA>EUR`, rate + in-transit + invoices), index. The tape (`world.ts`) records what every kind's `chart` needs (markets, pools, cohorts, occupations, curve tenors, central-bank lines, banks, funds, books). **The functions** (`src/ui/functions/index.ts`, twenty-three): `overview` delegates to the module; `chart` draws any module's series; `peers` is the one SCREENER (`screener.tsx`) over the module's cohorts and columns, any column sortable both ways; `all` and `news` apply to every kind (`storyMentions` per module);
